Nature help

This guide holds the help that Springer Nature publishes for readers, authors, reviewers and librarians of Nature and its sister journals, gathered on 18 August 2026. The categories are the publisher’s own, taken from the trail printed above each article. Material written for librarians and institutions is kept under its own headings rather than left out, so a reader can skip it but does not lose it.

Access

Access Nature Masterclasses content

Access Nature Masterclasses content

Modified on: Mon, 30 Mar, 2026 at 4:32 PM

If you are looking for information concerning the Nature Masterclasses online courses, please visit our website nature.com or naturemasterclassesonline.cn within China.

Enter association code to activate Society access

Enter association code to activate Society access

Modified on: Mon, 29 Sep, 2025 at 7:36 AM

An association code (formerly known as tokens) is a long code incorporating symbols as well as alphanumeric characters. Because of its complexity we recommend to always copy and paste codes and not attempt to type them.

Before being able to activate your code you first need a Springer Nature Link account.

  1. Go to Springer Nature Link and log into your account.

  2. Once logged in you will see your registered name/account name displayed top-right-hand-side

  3. The association code field can be found in your profile under Account / “Manage your account” / Linked institutions:

You will then be connected to your organization’s account and you will have access to the content subscribed for by your organization.

Troubleshooting:

If you see the message: The code you have supplied does not appear to be valid. Please check and try again.

  • Please check that you have carefully copied and pasted the entire code including any leading symbols.
  • All our codes are valid for one activation only. If the association code has already been activated then the code will no longer be valid. If your association code does not appear to be valid please contact the society that provided you with the code or you can contact us at societyservice@springer.com.

Link Your Existing Nature Masterclasses Account with Your Institution’s Single Sign-On (SSO)

Link Your Existing Nature Masterclasses Account with Your Institution’s Single Sign-On (SSO)

Modified on: Mon, 30 Mar, 2026 at 8:42 PM

If you already have a Nature Masterclasses Account and want to use it with your institution’s subscription via SSO, follow these steps:

  • Search for your institution on this institutional search page, or here for users in China, to check how your institution provides access.
  • Select your institution and follow the on‑screen instructions. If your institution provides access to their subscription via SSO you will be directed to your institution’s Single Sign‑On portal.
  • Log in using your usual institutional credentials.
  • When redirected back to Nature Masterclasses, log in with your existing SN Account so your institutional affiliation can be associated with your NMO account.
  • Once logged in, you will be taken to the Nature Masterclasses On‑Demand platform with access to your institution’s subscription content.

Nature Masterclasses subscriptions

Nature Masterclasses subscriptions

Modified on: Thu, 26 Mar, 2026 at 5:44 PM

Subscriptions are available to Institutions, Departments and Labs*:

Institutional subscriptions: Institutions can purchase an institution-wide subscription. Institution-wide access is available via IP range, via SAML / Shibboleth or via mail domain. Request a quote.

Departmental subscriptions: Departments can purchase a group subscription for up to 100 individuals. Request a quote

Lab subscriptions: Labs can purchase a group subscription for up to 15 individuals. Request a quote

Individual subscriptions: These subscription types are not currently available. Recommend a subscription to your institution or department.

Nature Masterclasses website free registration benefits

Nature Masterclasses website free registration benefits

Modified on: Mon, 30 Mar, 2026 at 3:26 PM

By registering on Nature Masterclasses without a subscription, you get access to:

  • Two courses that are freely available: Focus on Peer Review and Research Integrity: Publication Ethics
  • Free samples of our portfolio of subscription only courses
  • Access to your certificates for courses you may have completed on your account while you had a subscription

Access methods

Associate users / set up remote access via a login account

Associate users / set up remote access via a login account

Modified on: Tue, 2 Jun, 2026 at 9:41 AM

Associated users is an “off campus” access method where patrons get access to your content by logging in to Springer Nature Link and nature.com with their Springer Nature account.

You can see and download a list of all associated users, and manually invite and remove users, via the Access page on the Librarian Portal.

Step 1: Sending an invitation to the user you want to associate with your institution account:

  • The registered administrator login to Springer Nature Librarian Portal

  • On the top panel, go to ‘Access’, ‘Associated users’. You will see ‘Associated users’:

  • Enter the email address(es) and select an end date, then click ‘Send invite’.

  • An email to ‘Get access’ will be sent to the email address(es) you entered. Step 2: Receiving an invitation email (sent in step 1).

Click on ‘Activate your access’ in the email and do one of the following:

If you are already logged in to your Springer Nature account: your account will be automatically associated – no need to do anything further.

If you are not currently logged in: you will be prompted to either log in or create a new account:

If you have an existing account: simply log in and you will then be automatically associated, as above.

If you do not have an account: simply sign up for one, once completed it will automatically be associated.

Once a new user has been associated they will (upon logging into their account) be able to access all of your organisation’s subscribed content.

Additional reading: Springer Nature Link / Springer Username and Password Help

Dynamic Cloud IP Addresses

Dynamic Cloud IP Addresses

Modified on: Thu, 9 Oct, 2025 at 9:30 PM

We can only allow the addition of static IP addresses on our platform. This is to ensure that access to purchased content and subscriptions can only be accessed by customers who hold a license.

There are security service companies that assigns IP addresses dynamically. Examples of such companies are Zscaler, Forcepoint, Netskope, Skyhigh Security, Musarubra Germany. We cannot support their IP addresses because they are frequently reassigned, which grants access to unknown institutions who have not paid for the content and would remove access from the institution who has paid for it.

Please consider an alternative authentication method, such as: SAML SSO, Athens, Referrer, Associated User.

Maintaining IP ranges

Maintaining IP ranges

Modified on: Thu, 14 Aug, 2025 at 6:29 PM

Go to the Access page on the Librarian Portal for a full list of the IP ranges from which your licensed content can be accessed.

If you wish to make any changes to these ranges, please contact customer support with the relevant information.

Please note we do not permit private or intranet IP addresses to be associated with a customer account. They are private and not discoverable in the public internet space so would not enable access as a user would never reach our sites via a private IP. The IP address you will see on your personal computer will be your private IP, but if you go to a site like whatismyipaddress.com you will see a different IP which is your public IP.

For more information please see Private IP Addresses: Everything You Need to Know.

Private IP Ranges are:

  • 10.0.0.0/8 IP addresses: 10.0.0.0 – 10.255.255.255

  • 172.16.0.0/12 IP addresses: 172.16.0.0 – 172.31.255.255

  • 192.168.0.0/16 IP addresses: 192.168.0.0 – 192.168.255.255 There are also Multicast IP addresses that are not used to route traffic. The range is:

  • 224.0.0.0 - 239.255.255.255 For more information on Multicast IP addresses, see Multicasting.&text=Multicast%20addresses%20cannot%20be%20used%20as%20source%20addresses%20for%20any%20traffic.)

Persisted Access

Persisted Access

Modified on: Fri, 26 Apr, 2024 at 5:36 PM

Persisted Access is mentioned here as a remote method. On that page you can also find an explanation as to why Persisted Access was introduced by viewing the article How the Pandemic has changed Authentication and Access

We are persisting institutional authentication in a user’s browser for 180 days since their last institutional access. When a user accesses a page, we look first for current institutional authentication (for example accessing via an institutional IP range, or a login via SAML). If no current authentication is found then we return the most recently persisted account number, which the user will have persisted against their session from their most recent authentication against an account.

Non institutional IPs can show on reports with usage. If a user has authenticated against an institutional account, and then in the same session on their browser accesses from a different location, then their session IP will change. This means a user accessing from home, for example, could have their home IP returned against an institutional authentication. Their authentication has persisted, but their location has changed, therefore picking up an ‘unknown to the institution’ IP when accessing the content.

Token URL Based Access for Nature and Scientific American

Token URL Based Access for Nature and Scientific American

Modified on: Wed, 22 Jul, 2026 at 3:43 AM

We highly recommend using IP-authenticated access because it guarantees unlimited access for all users within your institution. It also enables us to provide you with detailed monthly usage statistics reports. We protect the information (including IP addresses) in your registration, and do not give out or publish organizations’ IP addresses in any way.

If your organization is not able to use IP authentication, or if you need to enable remote access for Nature or Scientific American without a federated access method, you have the option of access via a token URL Token and Magic Word (password) combination. This method of access does not require IP authentication and enables remote access for your users from any computer.

To gain access users copy/paste the Token URL into a browser at any internet-enabled terminal.

Can my institution sign up for the Token based access model?

Yes. Please provide your institutions name, account number with us (if possible), your preferred MagicWord password, and a number that you would like us to set as the maximum number of users from your institution allowed to access at any one time. To request to be setup for the token based access model please contact customer support.

Can I still see the real-time customer usage for Token based access?

No, the Springer Nature Librarian Portal does not currently show how many customers are logged in. However, please be assured that no more than the maximum allowance will be logged in at any time.

How can I change my MagicWord for Token based access?

To change your MagicWord please contact customer support.

Immersive Reader in Microsoft Edge

Immersive Reader in Microsoft Edge

Modified on: Thu, 11 Apr, 2024 at 4:29 PM

If you are using Microsoft Edge, the new Immersive Reader may help enhance your reading experience.

Immersive Reader was originally designed to support readers with dyslexia and dysgraphia, but now is used by anyone who wants to make reading on their device easier.

Microsoft Edge Immersive Reader can help improve reading comprehension and enhance learning.

To enter Reader Mode, open the page you want to visit using Microsoft Edge. You can select Enter Immersive Reader in the address bar.

Select the text that you want to read, right-click, and from the context menu, select Open in Immersive Reader.

When you’re done using the reading tools, you can exit Immersive Reader by selecting Exit Immersive

Reader from the address bar.

Translate web pages in Immersive Reader

If you would like to translate a text into another language, please select Reading preferences. Choose your desired language and toggle Translate entire page.

Over 70 languages are currently available.

Please note that many of Springer Nature support articles are already available in multiple languages (German, Japanese and Chinese), so no additional translation tool is required to access them.

Read Aloud

select Read Aloud from the Immersive Reader toolbar.

To stop listening, select the Pause button or the X to close Read aloud.

Voice options allow you to change the reader’s voice and slow down or speed up their reading pace.

Text preferences in Immersive Reader

You can find variety of text and colour options such as Text size, Text spacing, and Page themes to enhance your experience in Immersive Reader.

AdisInsight Help and Support

“Research” noted in the Phase field

“Research” noted in the Phase field

Modified on: Fri, 3 Apr, 2020 at 3:58 PM

By our definition, research is the earliest stage of development that RDI covers. It is the very early stage of the program where bench analysis is taking place. This includes in-vitro work, target exploration, receptor binding studies, etc. Research is considered active development. It is the stage prior to pre-clinical work which is the next stage of development where molecules are tested in animals.

Conversely, inactive development includes suspended, discontinued or no development reported. Although, with ’no development reported, there may be development going on however, no progress has been reported for a substantial period.

AdisInsight’ s definition of a small molecule

AdisInsight’ s definition of a small molecule

Modified on: Fri, 3 Apr, 2020 at 3:47 PM

We define drugs as small molecules if their molecular weight is less than 800 Daltons.

We don’t follow Lipinski’s rule of 5 strictly because we find that many companies these days consider their drugs to fall into the small molecule category when they have molecular weights above that of 500 Daltons which is a criteria of Lipinski’s rule of 5.

Time length for drug to stay in AdisInsight if discontinued in all areas of development

Time length for drug to stay in AdisInsight if discontinued in all areas of development

Modified on: Fri, 3 Apr, 2020 at 4:31 PM

Once a drug is added to our database, it remains in our database indefinitely.

AOP affects related to press releases and embargos

AOP affects related to press releases and embargos

AOP version and volume/issue/page (‘print’) version defined

AOP version and volume/issue/page (‘print’) version defined

Modified on: Fri, 5 Apr, 2024 at 10:00 AM

For convenience, the PDF version of every AOP article is given a temporary pagination, beginning with page 1. This is unrelated to the final pagination in the printed article.

The AOP version is identical to the final online version in every respect regarding content and presentation, except that instead of having a volume/issue/page number it has only the DOI as an identifier.

More often, associated News and Views articles are not published until the papers are published in the print/online edition of the journal.

Nature occasionally publishes other article types AOP, for example News and Commentaries.

If you are interested in submitting any of the above to us, please go to submissions to Nature Journal for additional information.

Author Copyrights information

Author Copyrights information

Modified on: Wed, 24 Dec, 2025 at 6:59 AM

Authors retain copyright of their research manuscripts and receive clear credit for, and ownership of their work. Generally, authors will be asked to grant non-exclusive rights to Springer Nature to publish, reproduce, copy and distribute. Please see our policy page for more information.

By opting for open access in fully OA or hybrid journals, authors agree to publish their article(s) under the Creative Commons Attribution License CC BY (as of January 16, 2012). Articles published before that date are subject to the Creative Commons Attribution Non-commercial License CC BY NC.

CC BY NC is also used by some of our Adis journals, so please check journal(s) page for details. Re-use of open access material is allowed without additional permissions provided that an appropriate credit to the original author(s) and the source is given, as well as link to the Creative Commons licence and any changes made are clearly indicated.

Articles published under subscription model cannot be shared without official permissions. Copyright Clearance Center’s RightsLink® service makes it faster and easier to secure permission for the reuse of Springer Nature content. To seek permissions, please visit Springer Nature Link or Nature.com and locate the desired article or book chapter that contains the material you wish to reuse. Go to the article or chapter page you wish to reuse content from. (Note: permissions are granted on the article or chapter level, not on the book or journal level). Scroll to the bottom of the page, or locate via the side bar, the “Reprints and Permissions” link at the end of the chapter or article.

If you are are unable to use this service or have other questions, please contact our teams at journalpermissions@springernature.com for reuse of journal content and bookpermissions@springernature.com for reuse of book content.

Visit Springer Nature’s copyright and piracy policies page if you need additional information.

Next steps for publishing your article: How to choose a publishing model (Open access or subscription)

Next steps for publishing your article: How to choose a publishing model (Open access or subscription)

Modified on: Tue, 15 Jul, 2025 at 7:57 PM

Following the acceptance for publication of your article after the Editorial process after submission, your manuscript is handed over to production for typesetting. Once typesetting is complete, we’ll guide you, the corresponding author, through simple, secure steps outlined in Next steps for publishing your article: What to expect after acceptance.

One of these steps will be to select a publishing model for your article. You will be asked to do so after confirming your country and affiliation.

If you are publishing in a hybrid journal (see Lists for Fully open access journals and hybrid journals), you can either select open access or non-open access (subscription) on a page that looks like this:

Please note that the APC prices mentioned above are example prices and not applicable to all journals (see Article Processing Charges (APC)).

Please check the conditions of your funding before choosing subscription. Some funders require immediate open access (read about open access policy requirements).

We encourage you to publish open access if you can — it makes your research more widely available. However, you will need to have the funds to pay the article processing charge (APC).

We suggest that you explore your funding options — it makes your decision easier. How to get information:

  • Check our list of funders first, as your APC funding may be covered
  • Consult our funding support service
  • Contact the librarian at your institution We know that this is not a quick decision — if you want to look for help to pay this before making a choice, you can return to this page when you’re ready.

Next steps for publishing your article: What to expect after acceptance

Next steps for publishing your article: What to expect after acceptance

Modified on: Mon, 1 Dec, 2025 at 7:58 AM

Following the acceptance for publication of your article after the Editorial process after submission, your manuscript is handed over to production for typesetting. Once typesetting is complete, we’ll guide you, the corresponding author, through simple, secure steps to:

  • Confirm your affiliation (institution or university), see also Next steps for publishing your article: How to confirm your affiliation
  • Choose whether to publish open access or non-open access (subscription), see also Next steps for publishing your article: How to choose a publishing model (Open access or subscription)
  • Determine whether an article processing charge (APC) is covered by an agreement from your institution
    • Accepted articles are checked for eligibility under our institutional open access agreements based on the institutional affiliation of the corresponding author. Some articles are eligible for part or full coverage of the APC as part of an institutional agreement with Springer Nature. In the event that an author is not eligible under an institutional open access agreement, they may still select the OA option and arrange for APC processing through direct payment or through a variety of funding options.
    • If you choose non-open access (subscription), there is no APCs to pay
  • Sign appropriate publishing agreement
    • Depending on the ownership of the journal and its policies, you will either grant the Publisher a non-exclusive license to publish the article or will be asked to transfer copyright of the article to the Publisher. A copy of the agreement is automatically sent to you via email for your records.
  • Arrange payment for associated publication costs (see APC Payment Options and Coloured article figures and Colour Charges) The article publication can only proceed once these guided steps are completed.

If you cannot press the next button, it might be that a mandatory field has not been filled out, such as the institution in the first step. If it still doesn’t work, please try using a different device.

If you have any questions along the way, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Page numbers in a Continuous Article Publishing (CAP) Journal

Page numbers in a Continuous Article Publishing (CAP) Journal

Modified on: Thu, 8 Jan, 2026 at 4:30 PM

CAP (continuous article publishing) means that each article, after peer-review and acceptance, will be published immediately within the monthly issue, including a digital object identifier (DOI) and page numbers. Due to this special structure each article starts with page 1.

Note: CAP Journals are not separated by volumes or issues. Instead, all articles are available individually, without separation into specific issues.

How to cite articles published in a Continuous Article Publishing (CAP) journal

Each article published in a CAP journal is assigned a unique article number, e. g. 62 in the case of:

“Dual-state dual emission from precise chemically engineered bi-ligand MOF free from encapsulation and functionalization with self-calibration model for visual detection” by Ameen, S.S.M., Qader, I.B., Qader, H.A. et al., published in Vol. 191 of Microchimica Acta.

This number is to be included in the citation alongside the volume number, e. g. Microchim Acta 191, 62 (2024).

The article number is distinct from the Digital Object Identifier (DOI), e. g.:

doi.org.

Timescale to publish an article for a Springer Nature journal

Timescale to publish an article for a Springer Nature journal

Modified on: Wed, 26 Nov, 2025 at 2:25 PM

It is not possible to put a definitive timescale on when an article will be published as there are many aspects in the process which are unpredictable. For example, although we try to limit the review period as much as possible we are highly dependent on the availability of reviewers and the time they are able to allot to each review, and therefore for a full research article the review process can typically take from 3 to 6 months.

If further information about the speed of publishing an article is available, it will be found on the journal’s page on Springer Nature Link. Please contact the journal for any submission related queries.

APC Discounts and Waivers

Access restricted error when requesting an article processing charge discount

Access restricted error when requesting an article processing charge discount

Modified on: Tue, 18 Nov, 2025 at 1:39 PM

When you try to request a discount for your article processing charge, you may encounter an ‘Access restricted’ error page. This is usually because you are logged in to the wrong Springer Nature account to make your request. To request a discount for your manuscript, you must be logged into an account with the same email address as the corresponding author email address entered during submission.

To check which account you are currently using, select the arrow icon next to your name.

If you are signed in with an email address that does not match the email address of the corresponding author used during submission, select ‘Log out,’ then log into the correct account and open the discount request again.

If you need to register a new Springer Nature profile, please follow the instructions in Register and login to Springer Nature Profile.

APC waivers and discounts

APC waivers and discounts

Modified on: Fri, 23 Jan, 2026 at 11:41 AM

APC waivers and discounts may be offered in cases of financial need for articles submitted to fully open access journals.

All applications*, for discretionary APC waivers should be made at the point of manuscript submission. Our online submission systems will allow you to indicate your interest in waivers or discounts. You may select a check box in the Declarations/Additional Information section that will direct you to the discount request screen. Be advised that this does not in-and-of-itself enter the request, you will need to later follow the link provided at the end of the submission process (and separately via email) to submit the request itself. To avoid delays, complete the discount request form within 14 days of submission. A decision on the discount will normally be made within two working days.

Please note that they will be considered on a case-by-case basis. For further details, please check our APC waivers and discounts policy.

*For fully Open Access Adis journals, please reach out to the Publishing Editor of the journal at the time of submission. Please check Find publishing contacts for Springer Nature journals.

I’ve been given an article processing charge (APC) token code. How do I apply this to my article?

I’ve been given an article processing charge (APC) token code. How do I apply this to my article?

Modified on: Tue, 18 Nov, 2025 at 1:39 PM

If your article is accepted for publication in a fully open access journal, you will get an email with a link to complete a publishing agreement. At the beginning of this process, after indicating your country / region of residence, you will be told the amount of the APC and be given an optional field to enter a token code. Please enter your code here and select ‘Apply’. Your discount or waiver will then be applied to the APC.

APC refund policy

APC refund policy

Modified on: Mon, 10 Feb, 2025 at 8:00 AM

Springer Nature will refund an APC if an error on our part has resulted in a failure to publish an article under the open access terms selected by the authors. This may include the failure to make an article openly available on the journal platform, or publication of an article under a different Creative Commons licence from that selected by the author(s). A refund will only be offered if these errors have not been corrected within 30 days of publication.

If you become aware of an error in the open access status or licensing of your article, please Contact Publishing Support immediately.

APCs will not be refunded when articles are retracted as a result of author error or misconduct.

APCs payment options

APCs payment options

Modified on: Mon, 10 Nov, 2025 at 8:11 AM

For articles accepted for publication, corresponding authors will receive an email, guiding them through the Next steps for publishing your article. If your selected journal is a fully Open Access journal or if you chose to publish Open Access in a hybrid journal, it will be determined if your institution has an agreement with Springer Nature. To check this at any time of the publishing process (including pre-submission), please visit our Funding for Open Access pages.

If your institution has an agreement with Springer Nature, the APC will either be partly or fully covered by them. If not, you will then be redirected to the payment page where you will either pay online by credit card payment or request an invoice for offline payment.

Online credit card payments are acknowledged immediately via an online receipt, followed by an email including a paid invoice PDF for reimbursement purposes around 3 - 5 days upon payment capturing.

If you wish to pay offline, you must first request an invoice. The invoice will include instructions for offline payment (credit card or bank transfer). Please note that while we can quote a purchase order number on your invoice, we do not accept purchase orders as a payment method and that offline invoice payments may incur an additional administrative charge. Once the invoice has been requested it cannot be reversed.

Invoice PDFs should arrive within 3 - 5 days of your online request. If it does not arrive within this time frame please contact Publishing Support. Please note that the invoice can only be emailed to the corresponding author.

Pay your invoice by credit card

Please follow the instructions for payment at the bottom of the invoice and contact the dedicated Credit Card email address, providing the number of the invoice you intend to pay for. Our Credit Card Team will then get in touch to organise the credit card payment with you. Please note that for PCI Compliancy purposes, we cannot accept credit card details by email, phone or fax.

Pay your invoice by bank transfer

Use the bank details which are located at the bottom of the invoice. Once payment is made simply email the invoice and your remittance slip to our AR Advice Team at ARadvice@springernature.com.

Currencies used for Open Access APCs, BPCs and CPCs

Currencies used for Open Access APCs, BPCs and CPCs

Modified on: Wed, 30 Oct, 2024 at 8:17 AM

Open access article processing charges (APCs), open access book processing charges (BPCs) and open access chapter processing charges (CPCs) are all charged in the following three currencies:

  • Great British Pound (£)
  • US Dollar (US$)
  • Euro (€) The currency you will be charged in is solely determined by the billing country provided and cannot be changed to accommodate fluctuating exchange rates.

The breakdown of countries for each currency is as follows:

  • United Kingdom: GBP (£)
  • The Americas*, Greater China** and Japan: USD (US$)
  • Rest of the world: EUR (€)
  • The Americas = USA, Canada, Central America and South America

** Greater China = Chinese mainland, Hong Kong, Macau and Taiwan

Identify scam invoices

Identify scam invoices

Modified on: Tue, 16 Jun, 2026 at 1:55 PM

When requesting a payment, Springer Nature will always either:

  • Send you an email with a link to one of our secure online payment platforms or
  • Send you an email with an invoice and/or our payment options using Springer Nature-branded stationery. If you have received a payment request which does not conform to the above (such as bank details in an email with an amount to pay but no Springer Nature invoice attached) you can contact the appropriate Customer Support team to confirm it is a genuine request from Springer Nature before making payment.

Modified on: Mon, 10 Feb, 2025 at 12:12 PM

Please note:
  • All documents will be issued in the same currency as the original invoice, regardless of the payer’s location.
  • If there is an administrative charge for offline payment on the original invoice, the same admin charge will be applied to each new invoice (50 GBP, 55 EUR or 75 USD, depending on the currency of the APC), as mentioned in APC Article Processing Charge Important Notes.
  • Please note that we can only remove taxes if billed to an institution/organisation with a valid VAT/GST/ABN/etc. number or a valid exemption certificate if applicable.
  • The amounts you would like to be applied to each document should total the APC price. (Taxes and admin fees will be applied separately on each document if applicable.)

Time period allowed for payment of an APC

Time period allowed for payment of an APC

Modified on: Thu, 30 Jul, 2020 at 6:41 PM

Credit card payments are processed immediately.

For invoice payments, the usual credit terms are 30 days from the original invoice’s date. Invoices will be chased periodically, but if after 60 days no payment is received, the author will be contacted.

Failure to pay invoices within the stated credit terms may result in restrictions placed upon authors’ ability to publish with Springer Nature in the future, involvement of a third party debt collection agency and legal proceedings.

However, Springer Nature recognizes that authors often do not pay themselves and, if an author’s institution or funder is to pay the APC, it may take some time for an invoice to be paid.

In the case of articles in journals Springer Nature publishes on behalf of partners, the partner organisation and, where appropriate, the journal editors will be notified of non-payment and will make a decision on what penalty to levy on the author.

Understanding PO Numbers for Invoices

Understanding PO Numbers for Invoices

Modified on: Mon, 1 Jun, 2026 at 9:25 PM

Company information for vendor registration of an OA article’s APC payment

Submitting an invoice can involve several pieces of information, and one detail you may be asked to provide is a purchase order (PO) number. While it may seem like a small addition, the PO number plays a significant role in ensuring your invoice is reviewed and approved without additional delays.

A PO number acts as a link between the institution’s internal purchase request and the invoice you submit, helping their finance team verify the charge and process payment efficiently.

So, what exactly is a PO number? Who is responsible for issuing it, and how do they benefit your invoicing process?

This article is written for authors and institutional administrators who are requesting or submitting invoices. It will therefore explain the purpose of PO numbers, why institutions rely on them, and how authors can use them correctly when preparing invoices. It also includes practical guidance to help you avoid common issues and ensure your invoice is processed efficiently.

  • What do I need to know about purchase orders?
  • What key information should each purchase order include?
  • What is a PO number?
  • Do I need a PO number?
  • What is the importance of a PO number in business transactions?
  • Who is responsible for providing the PO number?
  • What is the difference between an invoice number and a purchase order number?
  • How organisations create PO numbers
  • What are some common PO number formats?
  • Tips for using PO numbers correctly for invoices
  • Summary
What do I need to know about purchase orders?

Before looking at PO numbers, it helps to understand what a purchase order is. A purchase order (PO) is a document the buyer sends to the seller to confirm what they want to purchase. It outlines the key details of the order - such as the items or services requested, the quantity, the agreed price, and when they should be delivered.

Once the seller accepts the purchase order, it becomes a formal agreement between both parties. This shared record helps ensure everyone is clear on what has been approved and what should be supplied.

Consistent use of purchase orders ensures a smoother and more efficient procurement process. They help avoid misunderstandings, reduce errors, and provide a clear reference point when it comes time to issue or review an invoice. In short, POs help keep transactions organised from start to finish.

What key information should each purchase order include?

For a purchase order to function effectively, it needs to contain all the key details that outline the agreement between the buyer and the seller. This helps avoid confusion later and ensures both parties have a shared understanding of what has been approved.

A well‑structured purchase order typically includes:

  • Buyer and seller contact details
  • A PO number to identify and track the order
  • A description of the items or services, including quantities and prices
  • Payment terms, such as due dates or accepted methods
  • Delivery information, including the address and expected delivery date
  • Any additional terms or conditions relevant to the order These elements work together to create a clear, complete record of the transaction.
What is a PO number?

A PO number is a unique reference code assigned to a purchase order issued by the buyer. It helps both the buyer and the seller track the transaction throughout the procurement and payment process.

Organisations use PO numbers to match an invoice to the original order, confirm the agreed costs, and ensure the correct budget is being charged. This is why many institutions require the PO number to appear on the invoice before they can approve payment.

Do I need a PO number?

As previously mentioned, some institutions require a purchase order (PO) number before they can approve and pay an invoice.

If your institution uses PO numbers:

  • You must request the PO number from your finance or procurement team
  • You should provide the PO number when requesting or submitting the invoice If your institution does not use PO numbers, you can usually proceed without one.
What is the importance of a PO number in business transactions?

PO numbers do far more than label a document. When used consistently, they provide a clear link between what was ordered and what is being billed.

Here are some of the key reasons PO numbers are important:

  • Better tracking: A PO number makes it simple to locate and review a specific order, especially when dealing with large volumes of transactions.
  • Fewer errors: Matching a purchase order to its corresponding invoice helps prevent issues such as duplicate payments, incorrect deliveries, or disputes over charges.
  • Clear audit trails: PO numbers create a documented path from order to payment, supporting both internal checks and external audits.
  • Improved communication: When both the buyer and seller refer to the same PO number, it reduces the chance of confusion or misaligned information. In practice, the process starts when the buyer issues a purchase order for the goods or services they need. Once the seller fulfils the order, they send an invoice that should include the same PO number. This allows the buyer to quickly confirm that the invoice matches the original request.

Using PO numbers correctly helps streamline approvals, supports timely payments, and contributes to a smoother overall transaction for both parties.

Who is responsible for providing the PO number?

The PO number is always issued by the buyer - in this case, the institution or organisation paying the invoice fee. Authors do not create PO numbers themselves.

If your institution requires a PO number for payments, you will need to request it from your finance or procurement team before submitting your invoice. They will generate the purchase order and share the PO number with you.

What is the difference between an invoice number and a purchase order number?

Although PO numbers and invoice numbers may look similar, they serve different purposes in the purchasing and payment process.

  • PO number: Created by the buyer, this number is used to track the purchase order throughout the procurement workflow.
  • Invoice number: Issued by the seller, this number identifies the invoice itself and is used for billing, payment tracking, and maintaining accurate financial records. Understanding the difference between these two identifiers helps ensure transactions are recorded correctly and reduces the chance of confusion during approval or payment.
How organisations create PO numbers

Organisations typically create PO numbers in one of two ways:

  • Manual numbering: A team member assigns each new purchase order a number based on an internal format or sequence.
  • Automated numbering: Financial or procurement software generates PO numbers automatically according to predefined rules. The method an organisation chooses usually depends on its size, the volume of transactions it handles, and how structured its internal processes are. This means authors may see PO numbers in different formats depending on the institution, but the purpose remains the same: to identify and track the purchase order.
What are some common PO number formats?

PO numbers vary depending on the organisation’s internal system. Some examples include:

  • Sequential numbers: PO‑001, PO‑002
  • Date-based formats: 2026/PO‑001
  • Department-based codes: PO‑PUB‑001 (e.g., for the publishing department)
  • Project-based codes: PO‑PROJ1‑123 You do not need to interpret or modify the PO number - simply copy it exactly as provided.
Tips for using PO numbers correctly for invoices

When requesting or submitting an invoice:

  • Confirm whether your institution requires a PO number for the relevant payment

  • Request the PO number before submitting your invoice request

  • Enter the PO number exactly as provided - do not edit, abbreviate, or reuse a PO number

  • Ensure the PO number matches the purchase order issued by your institution

  • Keep a record of the PO number for reference if payment questions arise Invoice processing may be delayed if:

  • A required PO number is missing

  • The PO number on the invoice does not match the institution’s purchase order

  • An incorrect or outdated PO number is used Checking these details before submitting your invoice helps avoid delays.

Summary

The above content helps to explain how purchase orders and PO numbers are used in payment processes, including what they are, who issues them, and why some institutions require them. By outlining both the purpose of PO numbers and how they fit into institutional approval processes, this guidance is intended to give authors the context they need to understand requests for PO numbers and to provide them correctly when submitting or requesting an invoice.

Cover letter

Cover letter

Modified on: Tue, 29 Oct, 2024 at 9:11 AM

A well-written cover letter clearly explains why the submission will be of interest to the journal’s readers.

It should also be used to highlight any potential issues such as related manuscripts currently under consideration in any other Springer Nature publication, as well as indicating whether you have had any prior discussions with a Springer Nature editor about the work described in the manuscript.

It is an opportunity to declare that the manuscript is not being considered for publication in any other journal and recommend or exclude reviewers (including the reasons why).

Finally, it can be used to address any issues encountered while submitting the manuscript.

Cover letter checklist:

  • Check the journal’s Instructions for Authors for any cover letter requirements on the journals homepage.
  • Address the editor who will be assessing your manuscript by their name, if known.
  • Include the date of submission and the journal you are submitting to.
  • First paragraph: Include the title and article type (e.g. review, research, case study) and briefly explain the background and question you sought out to answer and why.
  • Second paragraph: Concisely explain what was done, the main findings and why they are significant.
  • Third paragraph: Indicate why the readers of the journal would be interested in the work by showing how your study fulfils the aims and scope of the journal and point out the importance of it.
  • To conclude, state the corresponding author and any journal-specific requirements that need to be complied with (e. g. ethical standards).
  • All cover letters should contain these sentences: “We confirm that this manuscript has not been published elsewhere and is not under consideration by another journal. All authors have approved the manuscript and agree with its submission to [insert the name of the target journal].” For more detailed information, please check How to submit a journal article: Cover letters.

For a complete tutorial on article submissions, please check Submitting a journal manuscript and peer review.

Embed figures within a manuscript’s text

First Author and Corresponding Author defined

First Author and Corresponding Author defined

Modified on: Sat, 12 Oct, 2024 at 4:46 AM

The first author is typically the primary contributor to a research paper. You may list multiple authors as first authors in the manuscript, mentioning in the author contribution statement that these authors contributed equally to the study.

The corresponding author takes responsibility for the manuscript during the submission, peer review and production process. All communication from submission to publication will be with the corresponding author.

On SNAPP, the requirement of managing all communication between the journal and all co-authors during submission and proofing may be delegated to a submitting author. In this case please make sure the Corresponding Author is clearly indicated in the manuscript. For more information, see Submitting and Corresponding Author roles on SNAPP.

The corresponding author does not need to be the first author or a senior author. The order of authors can be arranged during the submission process, in whichever order suits but submissions must be made by the corresponding author and not on behalf of the corresponding author.

Articles can be published with more than one corresponding author, but only one can be accommodated by the Peer Review System. For that, make sure that all corresponding authors are listed as such in your manuscript.

The logic behind only allowing a single author to access the manuscript is that only one author should take responsibility for the manuscript during the submission, peer review and production stages. Having a single point of contact ensures that the manuscript can progress rapidly through the process and that all communication from Springer Nature regarding a submission goes to a unique contact.

Number of authors who can contribute to a manuscript

Number of authors who can contribute to a manuscript

Modified on: Thu, 30 Jul, 2020 at 8:17 PM

An unlimited number of authors can contribute to a manuscript to be submitted to Springer Nature journals.

Preprints

Preprints

Modified on: Fri, 25 Oct, 2024 at 2:58 PM

Preprints are defined as an author’s version of a research manuscript prior to formal peer review at a journal, which is deposited on a public server (as described in Preprints for the life sciences. Science 352, 899–901; 2016).

Preprints may be posted at any time during the peer review process.

Posting of preprints is not considered to be a prior publication and will not jeopardize being considered for publication in Springer Nature journals.

Springer Nature has partnered with Research Square to provide In Review, a journal-integrated solution for preprint sharing. Please refer to our In Review page for more information.

APC Article Processing Charge Important Notes

APC Article Processing Charge Important Notes

Modified on: Wed, 9 Oct, 2024 at 4:53 AM

The following are the answers to the most frequently asked questions regarding APC payments.

Article Processing Charges (APC)

Article Processing Charges (APC)

Modified on: Fri, 13 Sep, 2024 at 5:50 PM

Authors are asked to pay an Article Processing Charge (APC) in order for their article to be published Open Access under a Creative Commons license. This covers the costs involved in every stage of the publication process, from administrating peer-review to copy editing and hosting the final article on dedicated servers. The APC is determined at the time of acceptance.

The cost of the APC is the same regardless of the number of words or the inclusion of figures. While this means that we do not charge extra for longer articles and place no limit on the number of figures, it also means that we cannot offer discounts for shorter pieces. Where a journal has variable APC depending on the article type, it is clearly stated on the journal’s website.

To find your journal’s APC, please check Find the APC for a specific journal.

For our hybrid journals, you can select the subscription route which means no APC will be charged.

Author’s payment of APCs does not influence acceptance of article’s publication

Author’s payment of APCs does not influence acceptance of article’s publication

Modified on: Mon, 4 Nov, 2024 at 9:14 AM

Please refer to our Editorial policies page within the Editorial Independence section.

Coloured article figures and Colour Charges

Coloured article figures and Colour Charges

Modified on: Mon, 4 Nov, 2024 at 9:22 AM

Colour charges are an additional fee charged by some of the journals in Springer Nature portfolio to article authors who wish to publish their figures in colour. They are independent of Article Processing Charges and therefore not covered by any institutional agreement.

For articles published in Springer journals

Authors who wish to publish their figures in colour in the printed version of their article will be given the opportunity to order colour in print after article acceptance. Please note that the electronic version of your article will always show coloured figures at no additional cost.

These costs may not apply to all Springer journals. Please check the submission guidelines on the homepage of the journal you are publishing with to see if your journal offers coloured figures in print.

For articles published in Nature and the Nature portfolio journals

Authors who wish to publish their figures in colour will be given the opportunity to order colour at final revision. Please note that where authors choose to publish open access, colour charges are included in the APC at no additional cost.

These costs only apply to journals which publish in both electronic and print; there are no colour charges for journals which do not publish in print.

Do Springer Nature’s article processing charges (APCs) offer good value?

Do Springer Nature’s article processing charges (APCs) offer good value?

Modified on: Mon, 30 Mar, 2026 at 2:12 PM

The APCs for our open access (OA) articles cover a range of services throughout every stage of the publication process, from peer-review to copyediting and hosting the final article on dedicated servers. In addition to this the charge ensures that an article is published open access under a Creative Commons license and made immediately available to all readers.

In most cases, fully open access journals published by Springer Nature do not charge additional fees for page or colour figure costs; all publication costs are included in the APC. Some titles owned by our society partners may charge page and colour charges in addition to the APC. Please check individual journal instructions for authors for further details.

Authors experiencing difficulties locating funds for OA article processing charges can get in touch with our OA Funding Policy Team for assistance with identifying potential sources of APC funding. Please see our contact page here: Contact Customer Support : Springer Nature Support. Alternatively, authors who wish to publish if fully OA journals who lack funds to pay APCs may request a discretionary waiver.

Please visit our OA and related publishing costs pages for additional information.

Download, print and troubleshoot Springer Nature PDF files

Download, print and troubleshoot Springer Nature PDF files

Modified on: Wed, 9 Oct, 2024 at 5:37 AM

Please note that the downloading of our PDF content is restricted to those users who have either a full subscription or Pay Per View access to their chosen journal or article.

Portable Document Format (PDF) provides a richer environment than other text formats and also allows materials to be printed exactly as the author(s) intended, including much of the supplementary information such as; drawings, 3D, maps, full-colour graphics, photos, tables, charts etc.

The PDF file can be easily downloaded to your computer, enabling you to view it offline at your convenience. Documents published in PDF format are typically much larger than simple text files. If your bandwidth is severely restricted you should consider the likely download time before attempting to retrieve such documents. In order to help you decide, we give the size of the PDF file alongside its description.

Letters to Journals, Research Articles and other editorial content are available to registered subscribers or institutional site license users as either Full Text (HTML) or as a PDF.

Downloading PDF files

In order to view PDF files, or save them for later use, please follow these simple steps:

  • Click on the PDF file link - your browser will automatically launch the Adobe Acrobat reader

  • Click the download icon - usually located in the top left or right corner of the screen dependent on which version you have installed

  • When you are done viewing the PDF file, click on your browser’s Back button to return the web page that had the PDF link Alternatively

  • Place the mouse cursor over a PDF file link, and then click your right mouse button. A small menu will appear

  • Select ‘Save Link As’ from the menu. A location box appears

  • Save the PDF file to a location you can remember. You can also save it is your Desktop In order to download and view our PDF files, it is important that you use Adobe Acrobat’s version 8.0 or above.

The Adobe Acrobat plugin needed to view a PDF is free to download. Clicking or double-clicking on the Acrobat file after it is downloaded will start installation of both a reader and a plugin on your system. (The reader enables you to view the file; the plug-in launches the reader automatically from your browser).

Printing PDF Files
  • Open a PDF file in your browser.
  • In the browser, select File and Print. The PDF document should print exactly as it looks on the screen.
PDF problems

We perform thorough checks to ensure that our PDF files are posted correctly.

Please click the relevant link below to view Adobe’s PDF troubleshooting tips:

Cannot View PDF in Browser

Error Message When Viewing PDF in Browser

Unable to Print PDF

Problems Downloading/Installing PDF Reader

If you continue to experience problems with downloading PDF files of Springer Nature content, please contact us through the webform.

If you would rather call your local Online Service team, their phone numbers are available on the Contact Us page.

Official publication date defined

Official publication date defined

Modified on: Wed, 20 Jan, 2021 at 5:11 PM

Many journals, and most abstracting and indexing services (including Medline and Institute for Scientific Information) still cite the print date as the publication date.

This is an evolving standard, however, and the trend now is for publishers to state both the ‘Online Publication Date’ and the ‘Print Publication Date’.

Nature Portfolio will continue to publish both dates for our own papers, and we hope that the scientific communities and abstracting and indexing services will recognize these dates.

For the time being, the reference lists of our papers will continue to follow the standard convention of citing by print publication date. Thus, there will be no immediate change to the reference format instructions in our Guides to Authors (but see below for how to cite papers that have not yet appeared in print).

We expect, however, to review this policy regularly as community standards evolve.

For legal purposes (for example, establishing intellectual property rights), it is our assumption that online publication will constitute public disclosure.

This is, however, for the courts to decide; our role as a publisher is to provide clear documentation of the publication history, online and in print

Purchasing an article on Nature.com

Purchasing an article on Nature.com

Modified on: Tue, 18 Feb, 2025 at 3:04 PM

Most articles published on nature.com are available for purchase on Springer Nature Link in in PDF format.

Nature.com

Under the Abstract details, the “Access Options” that are available for that article will be displayed. Select “Buy now” and you will be diverted to the Springer Nature Link version of the article.

Then select the “Buy article PDF” button displayed on the side of the Abstract when open on the Springer Nature link page and the article will be added to your cart ready for checkout.

You may select “Cart” to view the items within your cart or proceed with checkout at any time.

Please note the format of the article will look slightly different between the Nature.com and Springer Nature Link websites, but please be assured the content will remain the same.

Viewing the article on your bookshelf

Ensure you are logged into your MyNature account registered to the same email address that was used to purchase the article.

Select “Subscriptions and purchased” from the Account overview page

Select “View eBookshelf”

This will direct you to the saved and purchased digital copies of books, articles, and chapters. Here you will be able to select the title which is hyperlinked to the articles abstract, or you can download a PDF of the item at any time.

As part of purchasing Nature research articles through the website, you should receive an order confirmation email shortly after with a downloadable link to a PDF of the article you have purchased. If you do not receive this, please check the eBookshelf first before contacting our Customer Service team.

Unfortunately, it is not possible to purchase articles by phoning Springer Nature, nor rent or purchase articles through ReadCube from May 2024.

Supplementary information

Supplementary information

Modified on: Sat, 12 Oct, 2024 at 3:57 AM

Supplementary Information is additional data supplied by some authors to accompany articles published in the printed journal and is not provided for all articles. Please also note that supplementary information is unavailable online from some articles published prior to 2000.

Supplementary information can take various forms such as data sets, tables, additional figures or images and audio or movie files. Links to the supplementary information are located on the full text or abstract pages of the article it is associated to. It is however usually incorporated into the PDF version of articles, although accompanying audio and movie files will be available as separate downloads.

You may need a current subscription to the journal in which the Supplementary Information appears in order to access it. If you find any supplementary information which you are unable to open or download you can submit a query to our Online Service team via our webform here. If you would rather email or phone them, their contact information is available here. Please provide the details of the article including title and URL to allow us to resolve the issue as quickly as possible.

Frequently Asked Questions - Get Found Faster - Book ORCIDs 2025

Frequently Asked Questions - Get Found Faster - Book ORCIDs 2025

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 3:32 PM

Frequently Asked Questions - Get Found Faster - Book ORCIDs 2025

Why are we doing this?

Springer Nature is updating the metadata of thousands of book publications from the past 14 years.

A key part of this update is adding Persistent Identifiers for originators like ORCID iDs to ensure authors are uniquely identifiable and their work is properly indexed and discoverable across systems.

Why did we contact you?

You are listed as an author or editor of one or more books published by Springer Nature.

For at least one of these titles, we do not yet have an ORCID on file for you.

What is ORCID?

ORCID stands for Open Researcher and Contributor ID.

It is a unique, persistent identifier that distinguishes you from other researchers and connects your work across systems and platforms.

Learn more on orcid.org

How will my ORCID be used?

Once submitted, your ORCID will be:

  • added to the metadata of your books
  • shared with trusted external partners (e.g., libraries, retailers, indexing services)
  • displayed on Springer Nature Link How can I ensure my ORCID record is updated correctly?

To allow Springer Nature to update your ORCID record:

  • Your ORCID profile must be set to public visibility
  • You must grant permission via a notification email from ORCID after your publication is registered What happens next?

We aim to complete this metadata update by the end of 2025.

In the unlikely event that we encounter any issues matching your ORCID to your publications, we may contact you again.

What if I don’t have an ORCID?

You can register for free at: orcid.org.

It only takes a minute, and you’ll receive a permanent identifier for your scholarly work.

What if I use a different email address?

Please use the same email address you received this message on.

If you submit a different address, we may not be able to match your ORCID to your book(s) correctly.

Why did you contact me on that email address? It doesn’t belong to me.

No action is required. Unless you fill in the form, we will not add an ORCID in this campaign.

Why do you mention this book, but not others?

We mention only one example to avoid long lists, and it could also be that another book has been published using another email address.

What if I don’t respond?

Participation is voluntary. However, some of our partners now require persistent identifiers like ORCID for proper indexing and metadata delivery.

Without one, your book may not be fully visible or correctly attributed in their systems.

How is my data protected?

  • The submission form is hosted internally via Microsoft Forms and is encrypted.
  • Your ORCID and email address will only be used for the purpose of updating your author metadata.
  • Data will not be shared beyond the systems and trusted partners involved in this project.
  • All data is handled in accordance with the EU General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR).
  • Learn more: Springer Nature Privacy Policy What if I have more questions?

For queries, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Guide to Accessing Your eBook Performance Data

Guide to Accessing Your eBook Performance Data

Modified on: Wed, 25 Feb, 2026 at 3:54 PM

Springer Nature is committed to providing authors with clear and accessible information about their book’s visibility and impact. This page explains how you can find performance data for your book.

Overview

Springer Nature previously provided an annual eBook Performance Report. This report was discontinued in 2020 and is no longer available in its original form. However authors continue to have access to key performance metrics online, and more detailed insights can be requested through our customer support team.

Where to Find Basic Performance Metrics

You can access essential performance metrics directly on your book’s Springer Nature Book Page. These include:

  • Accesses
  • Citations
  • Altmetric These metrics are available for all published books and update automatically as new data becomes available via Home | Springer Nature Link.

The screenshot below show an example of where to find this data:

Requesting a Custom Report

If you require more insight into your Book’s performance, you may request a custom report. These reports are prepared manually and it includes:

  • Yearly Trends
  • Monthly Trends
  • Books by chapter
  • Usage by Country
  • Region - Books by title To request this report, please use our Book Authors Support form.

Information on Author Discount

Information on Author Discount

Modified on: Tue, 7 Jul, 2026 at 2:39 PM

-Book authors, editors and chapter authors Co-Author, Editor-in-Chief or Editorial Board Member are entitled to order Springer Nature books — print and electronic versions — at a special discount.

-The discount can be used by one person only. It is valid for a lifetime.

-In many cases your author discount can be combined with other discounts, so that everyone can benefit from our special offers. However, 50% discount campaigns are excluded. For details, please carefully review the conditions of each coupon that you receive from us.

-Author discounts are only applicable for book purchases. Authors who want to subscribe to journals or purchase single issue orders are not entitled to an author’s discount.

-An author discount will only be allowed if purchased directly by the author, in the author’s name and using the author’s billing address details. A purchase with author discount cannot be made in the author’s Institution/Company name, because the Institution/Company is a separate entity.

-Author purchases are not eligible for tax exemption status (even though your Institution/Company may be), since authors are classified as individuals; and by law, individuals are not tax-exempt. Tax-exempt certificates from organizations will not be honored on author orders. Sales tax will be applied, where applicable.

-The quantity of books ordered must be in the normal range for private use and the resale of the books is not permitted.

If you would like to place an order using your author discount refer to How to get your author discount on our Springer Nature Link webshop.

The form will ask for your Business Partner (BP) ID and debug results which you can obtain by following the steps in our Site License Access Issue Help article. The Business Partner (BP) ID also be located by navigating to our Librarian Portal.

The form also asks for a copy of the fully executed license agreement if you have it available for upload.

Journal Issue Claims Form

Journal Issue Claims Form

Modified on: Wed, 25 Mar, 2026 at 5:47 PM

If you are missing one or more issues from your subscription to a Springer Nature journal or have received a damaged issue, please fill in the required details in the form below so we can handle your claim as quickly as possible.

Note: If you are claiming the first issue of a new weekly subscription, the initial issue may take between 2 to 4 weeks of the order being placed for it to arrive.

Weekly titles claim policy: We will replace issues of weekly journals if you contact us to claim the missing issues within two months of the date of publication.

Monthly titles claim policy: We will replace issues of monthly journals if you contact us to claim the missing issues within three months of the date of publication.

In cases where the issue has since gone out of print, we regret that we will be unable to replace, refund or extend your subscription.

In cases where your new subscription includes back issues that are out of print, we will automatically extend your subscription for the number of back issues you are owed.

Unfortunately we cannot offer compensation for supplements that are out of print as supplements are not included in the subscription pricing.

Replacement issues are subject to availability and are limited to three replacements.

Customers in North, South and Latin America

For customer data security, Springer Nature offers the possibility to pay proforma invoices and invoices with credit card via a secure payment link. For Proforma invoices, send your request to our Credit Card Proforma payment link email address: creditcardprofus@springernature.com. Include the proforma invoice or invoice number and the customer account number within your request.

For Invoices, send your request to our Credit Card payment link email address: creditcard@springernature.com. A secure payment link, which will enable you to submit the payment with a credit card, will then be sent to your email address.

Customers located in Rest of World (ROW)

For customer data security, Springer Nature offers the possibility to pay proforma invoices and invoices with credit card via a secure payment link. For Proforma invoices, send your request to our Credit Card Proforma payment link ROW email address: creditcardprofrow@springernature.com. Include the proforma invoice and the customer account number within your request.

For Invoices, please send your request to our Credit Card payment link ROW email address: creditcard@springernature.com. A secure payment link, which will enable you to submit the payment with a credit card, will then be sent to your email address.

Content and tools

Content on Demand

Content on Demand

Modified on: Mon, 31 Mar, 2025 at 1:53 PM

Content on Demand (CoD) is a cross-platform solution that provides institution-wide access to all unlicensed Springer Nature content, as available, with the use of prepaid sets of downloads at a fixed cost-per-unit.

Download packages start from 150 units up to 5,000 or more.

Options:

  • Springer Nature CoD: use the same set of downloads to access resources on Springer Nature Link and nature.com CoD concurrently, based on the same terms and conditions.
  • Springer Nature Link CoD: access Springer Nature Link resources only
  • nature.com CoD: access nature.com resources only A dedicated admin account and custom usage reports are available to help you monitor your account balance.

You can add units to your account at any time.

Subscription, Open Access and free content will not be deducted from your download total.

Licensed downloads remain valid for 2 years.

Please contact your Licensing manager for more information.

Content on Demand - your Librarian Portal account

Content on Demand - your Librarian Portal account

Modified on: Fri, 20 Jun, 2025 at 6:01 AM

  • Content on Demand units & remaining balance
  • Content on Demand Usage reports
  • User interface settings
  • Download Definition AS CoD admin you can log into Springer Nature’s Librarian Portal to manage your account, check your remaining units, download your usage report and enable additional settings for your users.
Content on Demand units & remaining balance

You can monitor your CoD licenses, remaining units and account expiration via this section:

Content on Demand Usage reports

You can download your CoD usage in csv format, at your convenience. Please note that usage reports are custom to CoD and not COUNTER-compliant.

User interface settings

You may enable a setting to notify users, affiliated with your institution, that they are accessing content on demand prior to downloading or viewing any content material in full-text.

As an additional optional setting, your users may be also prompted to enter their cost code or department prior to downloading or viewing any material in full-text. The information captured will be referenced in your CoD usage report.

To enable these options please visit to the Account settings section of the Librarian Portal:

Your configured settings will influence what your users see on the platform when they access content available via CoD. If the above section remains unticked, then users will be accessing content on our platforms seamlessly.

‘Full text access confirmation’:

‘Full-text access confirmation with optional department/cost code’:

Download Definition
  • A download (html view or pdf) of a journal article, eBook chapter, reference entry or Protocol chapter will deduct one CoD unit from your account.
  • A download of an entire eBook in PDF or ePub will deduct 7 CoD units, at a maximum. Download term: Unlimited downloads of the same content material (DOI) by any entitled user at your organisation, within a period of 12 hours, will only deduct one unit from your total.

Institutional site license consortia features

Institutional site license consortia features

Modified on: Tue, 28 Oct, 2025 at 10:56 AM

If your institution is interested in purchasing an institutional site license via a consortium, Springer Nature is here to help. We work with a variety of consortia and networks to provide flexible and customised solutions for your institution’s needs.

When working with a consortium, Springer Nature offers the following features:

  1. Flexibility in offering customised pricing and titles for each member of the consortium. This allows each member to access the content they need at a price that works for their budget.

  2. Access to either aggregated usage statistics or separate statistics per member. This allows for easy tracking of usage and ensures that each member of the consortium can monitor their own usage of the content.

  3. Specialised customer service to assist with any questions or issues that may arise during the purchasing process or while using our content.

For more information on how Springer Nature can work with your consortium, please visit the Springer Nature Librarian Gateway. If you require further assistance, you can submit a query to our Institutional Support team via our webform.

For all questions regarding consortia arrangements, please reach out to the Springer Nature sales team. Contact information can be found on the Librarian Resource Page.

We are committed to providing the best possible service to our consortium partners and look forward to working with your institution to provide access to our content.

License single eBooks in the Librarian Portal

License single eBooks in the Librarian Portal

Modified on: Wed, 10 Dec, 2025 at 8:02 AM
