Institutional Access Troubleshooting Guide

Institutional Access Troubleshooting Guide

Modified on: Wed, 5 Aug, 2026 at 6:31 PM

STEP 1 – Check Access Basics
  • Is the content covered by your institution’s subscription (including applicable titles and copyright years)?
  • Is the issue affecting a single article or multiple articles, or is it a platform-wide access problem?
  • If the issue is limited to a single article, do you encounter a paywall, see only the abstract, or are you unable to access the full text of the article? *Please note that some older content may not be available in digital format.
  • Is your institution correctly licensed or covered under the appropriate consortium membership?
STEP 2 – Verify Authentication Settings
  • Are you using the correct access method (IP authentication or Single Sign-On (SSO))?
  • Are you accessing the content from a registered IP address range?
  • Is SSO properly configured and currently active?
  • Do you receive any error messages when attempting to sign in? View access methods on the Springer Nature Librarian Portal
STEP 3 – Quick Fixes
  • Clear your browser cache and cookies
  • Try accessing the content using a different browser or device
  • Verify your proxy or remote access configuration Site License Access Issue Help
STEP 4 – Still Experiencing Issues?

Submit an Institutional Online Access Issue Form

*The form is available in English, German, Chinese Simplified, and Japanese. A drop-down menu is available in the upper-right corner of the form, allowing you to select your preferred language.

Please include a screenshot, URL, institution details, product information, and a description of the issue.

Please also provide the date and time of the access attempt, as this information may help our technical team investigate the issue more effectively using system logs.

Librarian Portal

Librarian Portal

Modified on: Fri, 6 Feb, 2026 at 1:47 PM

The Springer Nature Librarian Portal provides librarians and administrators with a central location to manage their Springer Nature licensed content.

We have developed the Librarian Portal in order to provide administrators with a simple solution for managing users, accessing title lists and monitoring usage statistics across both Springer Nature Link and nature.com licensed content.

Librarian Portal Chatbot

Librarian Portal Chatbot

Modified on: Fri, 19 Dec, 2025 at 12:52 PM

Understanding Higgs: Your Friendly Chatbot on the Librarian Portal

Welcome to our knowledge base article about Higgs, the chatbot designed to assist institutional and corporate administrators on the Librarian Portal. We understand that navigating licensed content can sometimes be challenging, and that’s where Higgs comes in to help!

Access Higgs on the Librarian Portal, or click the “Connect with Higgs for Librarians” button appearing before the heading of this article.

What is Higgs?

Higgs is an intelligent chatbot specifically tailored to support administrators with inquiries related to licensed content. Whether you have questions about access, usage rights, or any other licensing issues, Higgs is here to provide you with the information you need.

How Does Higgs Work?

Higgs focuses on suggesting relevant support articles based on the questions you ask. Simply type your query into the chat, and Higgs will analyze your request to provide you with the most pertinent articles from our extensive knowledge base. This means you can quickly find the answers you need without having to sift through multiple resources. The goal is to streamline your experience and ensure you have access to the right information at your fingertips.

We would value any feedback you provide regarding the information supplied by Higgs when asked, as it will assist us in enhancing functionality.

Important Note for Students and Researchers

If you are a student or researcher, we kindly ask that you use our webform link instead of the chatbot. This is to ensure that your inquiries are directed to the appropriate support channels, allowing us to assist you more effectively. The webform is designed to capture the specific details of your request, ensuring that you receive the best possible support tailored to your needs.

In summary, Higgs is here to make your experience on the Librarian Portal as smooth as possible. If you’re an administrator with questions about licensed content, feel free to engage with Higgs for quick and relevant support. For students and researchers, don’t hesitate to reach out through our webform for dedicated assistance. Thank you for using our services!

Log in and Registration

Log in and Registration

Modified on: Mon, 13 Oct, 2025 at 8:07 AM

Will my Nature Site License Administration credentials still work?

No, unfortunately these credentials do not work for the Springer Nature Librarian Portal. However, you can use your Springer credentials. If you don’t have a Springer account go to librarian.springernature.com and follow the registration instructions.

Will my Springer Admin Portal credentials still work?

Yes, Springer Admin Portal credentials work for the new Springer Nature Librarian Portal.

I can’t remember my password for Springer Admin Portal.

For instructions on resetting your password, go to the Forgotten Springer Nature Profile password page.

I don’t have a user account. What should I do?

To request admin access, please submit a ticket to our Institutional Support team.

I cannot locate my registration email.

Please submit a ticket to our Institutional Support team to request a new registration email.

Licencing & Copyright

‘Work for hire’ Licence to Publish / Copyright (Employer owns the copyright)

‘Work for hire’ Licence to Publish / Copyright (Employer owns the copyright)

Modified on: Fri, 5 Jul, 2024 at 3:55 PM

If you do not own the rights to your contribution to an article because the paper derives from a work conducted within your employment, a non-standard licence agreement is required.

In this “work for hire” case, the employer is the Rights Holder and owns the copyright.

Please request a Non-Standard Copyright.

Can OA content published under a CC BY-NC-ND licence be posted on scholarly sharing platforms?

Can OA content published under a CC BY-NC-ND licence be posted on scholarly sharing platforms?

Modified on: Tue, 16 Jul, 2024 at 10:37 AM

Links to CC BY-NC-ND content can be posted to any location, including commercial platforms like social media or commercial scholarly sharing platforms, enabling users to easily discover OA content.

Posting copies of the full text of CC BY-NC-ND content to commercial platforms is not supported by the non-commercial terms of the licence chosen by the authors, and requires permission from Springer Nature. To request permissions please find the Rights and Permission section of the article you are interested in sharing and follow the instructions.

Alternatively please contact our Permissions team.

For more information about Permissions please visit our Rights, Permissions, Third Party Distribution section.

Crown copyright

Crown copyright

Modified on: Fri, 5 Jul, 2024 at 3:52 PM

In the United Kingdom, Canada and Australia, the copyright of articles produced by civil servants, ministers and employees of government departments and agencies in the course of their duties belong to the Crown.

Government departments do not own copyright in their own right.

Copyright can also come into Crown ownership when the legal owner assigns or transfers the copyright to the Crown; for example, when a government department commissions a person or organisation to produce a report on its behalf.

When the corresponding author goes through the Next steps for publishing your article, they will be asked to agree to a license. There will be an opportunity to select the option of Crown copyright and the correct licence agreement will be generated.

For other copyright scenarios, see information on Non-Standard Copyright.

Do I need to transfer copyright to Springer Nature if I publish open access?

Do I need to transfer copyright to Springer Nature if I publish open access?

Modified on: Mon, 10 Feb, 2025 at 11:41 AM

Copyright to any open access article is retained by the author(s).

Authors grant the publisher a license to publish the article and identify itself as the original publisher. Authors also grant any third party the right to use the article freely as long as its integrity is maintained and its original authors, citation details and publisher are identified.

In instances where authors are not allowed to retain copyright to their own article (where the author is a US Government employee for example), authors should Contact Publishing Support before submitting their article so we can advise as to whether their Non-Standard Copyright request can be accommodated.

For more information about copyright and licensing for each of our open access brands, please see the links below:

  • BMC
  • SpringerOpen
  • Nature Portfolio
  • Palgrave Macmillan
  • Springer

European Union (EU) Copyright

Modified on: Fri, 5 Jul, 2024 at 3:58 PM

If the copyright is owned by the EU, a non-standard copyright is required.

When going through the Next steps for publishing your article, you will need to request a Non-Standard Copyright. The licence agreement will need to be signed by both the legal representative and the corresponding author.

Intergovernmental Organisation (IGO) Licence and Copyright

Intergovernmental Organisation (IGO) Licence and Copyright

Modified on: Wed, 30 Oct, 2024 at 2:23 PM

If you do not own the rights to your contribution to an article because the paper derives from a work conducted within your employment with an IGO, a non-standard licence agreement is required.

Licence: All Springer Nature journals with an open access option offer intergovernmental organisation (IGO) versions of Creative Commons licences on request, where required by the author’s employer.

Copyright: When an IGO owns the copyright, you should not sign a standard licence agreement during the Next steps for publishing your article. Instead, you will need to request a Non-Standard Copyright and the correct agreement will be created for you manually.

If you are publishing open access, an IGO licence will also be applied at the same time as the Non-Standard copyright.

Meeting funder and institutional OA licence requirements

Meeting funder and institutional OA licence requirements

Modified on: Mon, 21 Jul, 2025 at 8:32 AM

Authors retain copyright of all Open Access (OA) articles published by Springer Nature.

Authors publishing their articles OA will sign an OA licence to publish agreement (LTP), and grant non-exclusive rights to Springer Nature where a CC BY licence is selected, or exclusive commercial and derivative rights where CC BY-NC-ND is chosen. See also What are the differences between CC BY and CC BY NC ND licences?.

Authors are advised to check their funder’s open access (OA) requirements, to ensure compliance.

Certain funders require grantees to publish with a specific OA licence, CC BY.

Check our list of funders with Creative Commons licence requirements, and learn more about funder OA requirements.

Where the costs of OA publication are covered by an institutional or funder OA agreement, the OA licence options available to authors may vary according to the agreement terms, many of the agreements mandate the use of CC BY licence.

Open Government Licence (OGL)

Open Government Licence (OGL)

Modified on: Wed, 30 Oct, 2024 at 8:59 AM

The Open Government Licence is a default copyright license for Crown copyright works.

This licensee permits anyone to copy, publish, distribute, transmit and adapt the licensed work provided that the source of the work is correctly cited.

Publishing agreements for authors affiliated with US Government Contractors and US National Laboratories

Publishing agreements for authors affiliated with US Government Contractors and US National Laboratories

Modified on: Thu, 26 Feb, 2026 at 1:25 PM

Journal authors affiliated to US Government Contractors who are prevented from personally signing our standard publishing agreements and require a non-standard copyright line to be added to their article upon publication should complete the Non Standard Copyright request PDF form as soon as possible after submission of their article.

Upon receipt of this request, our Author Service team will:

  • Arrange for (a) non-standard publishing agreement(s) to be generated and signed*.

  • For articles submitted to a

    • Subscription or fully open access journal**, liaise with the Editorial Office to request that the standard publishing agreements are not sent to authors upon editorial acceptance.
    • Hybrid or Transformative journal***, ensure the system handling publishing agreements and payments after editorial acceptance bypasses the standard publishing agreements.

  • Liaise with the journal’s Editorial and Production team to ensure they are aware of the required non-standard copyright line to be used upon publication of the article.

  • Confirm to the author that the non-standard requirements have been shared with the journal and that authors are advised to ensure they have been implemented upon checking the proofs for their article. The publishing agreement generated will include a clause voiding it, should the submission made not be accepted for publication and will also accommodate small changes to the title of the article throughout the peer-review process.

  • Where not all authors of the article are affiliated to the US Government Contractor, separate publishing agreements will be generated and sent for signature to the relevant signatory.

  • Please note that some fully open access journals require the submitting author to tick a box agreeing to the standard publishing agreement as part of the submission process. In such instances, authors should tick this box to be able to proceed and the values will then be manually overridden by our Author Service team.

  • The publishing agreement which will be generated differs depending on the publishing model chosen for the article. The form below requires authors to indicate which publishing model they intend to have their article published under to that effect. Please note that this choice will be entered into the system, meaning the author will no longer have the option to choose a publishing model after editorial acceptance.

  • The Non Standard Copyright request PDF form is only available in English. If you are unable to complete the form since it is only available in English, please contact Publishing Support.

Standard and Non-Standard Copyright

Standard and Non-Standard Copyright

Modified on: Fri, 17 Jul, 2026 at 1:08 PM

Do I need a non-standard copyright?

The copyright line determines who has the legal right to the article after it is published. A ‘standard’ copyright usually lies with the author(s); however, there are several exceptions to this. If any of the authors do not hold the rights to their own contributions, then a Non-Standard Copyright may be needed.

Part of the publication process will involve the corresponding author going through the Next steps for publishing your article during which they will be asked to agree to a license.

The following scenarios are considered ‘standard’. As you work through the online publishing workflow, you can select the appropriate option and a licence will automatically be generated.

As part of our unified solution, we offer licenses to publish for the following scenarios as standard:

A ‘standard’ author

The author(s) owns the rights to their own contributions in the article

Crown

In the United Kingdom, Canada and Australia, the copyright of articles produced by civil servants, ministers and employees of government departments and agencies in the course of their duties belong to the Crown. Government departments do not own copyright in their own right.

Most Federal US Government Employees

The US government may not own rights due the public domain status of the material, but an agreement must still be signed.

Federal US Government employees who have the right to sign their own agreement may select this option.

*Except for NIH/CDC/FDA and NASA who will need to make a non-standard request

If all authors are included in one of the above scenarios, then it is not necessary to request a non-standard copyright.

If any of the Rights Holders belong to one of the below categories, we will need to use a manual process to generate a licence agreement.

Common examples of non-standard copyright

US Government contractors

(e.g. organisations running US national laboratories)

Authors affiliated to US Government Contractors who are prevented from personally signing our standard publishing agreements will require a non-standard form.

NIH, CDC, and FDA Employees

As a NIH employee you are not permitted to transfer your copyright to the publisher, but we have a solution in place which will ensure your compliance.

Please Contact Publishing Support with a NIH cover sheet for assistance. This is a copy of the NIH coversheet; the CDC and FDA cover sheets will look similar to this

NASA Employees

When NASA owns the copyright, you should not sign a standard licence agreement. Instead please request a non-standard copyright.

“Work for hire” (employer owns the copyright)

If you do not own the rights to your contribution because the paper derives from a work conducted within your employment, a non-standard agreement is required.

In this “work for hire” case, the employer is the Rights Holder and owns the copyright.

European Union (EU)

If the copyright is owned by the EU, a non-standard form will need to be signed by both the legal representative and the corresponding author

Intergovernmental Organisations

An Intergovernmental Organisation (IGO) is an organisation composed primarily of sovereign states or of other intergovernmental organisations, which has been set up to carry out projects and plans in common interest.

Common examples include the World Bank, IMF and NATO.

How and when do I request a non-standard copyright?

Once your article has been accepted and you determine that a non-standard copyright is required, please fill in the attached form and send it to us via Contact Publishing Support. Please wait and do not complete the Next steps for publishing your article until OR Support instructs you to proceed.

From there, the process will follow these steps:
  • OR Support will review your request form and liaise with our Editorial Rights team in order to create the licence agreement(s). This may take a few business days depending on the complexity and the type of agreement(s) required.
  • Most license agreements will be sent to the designated signatory through DocuSign.
  • The OR Support team will then instruct you how to complete the Next steps for publishing your article and will send the completed licence agreements and agreed copyright line to the production editor.
  • The Production Editor will then oversee the final steps of the publication process.
For details on each specific type of copyright, please refer to the support articles below.
  • Do I need to transfer copyright to Springer Nature if I publish open access?
  • ‘Work for hire’ Licence to Publish : Springer Support
  • Intergovernmental organisation (IGO) licence : Springer Support
  • Crown copyright : Springer Support
  • European Union (EU)
  • US Government Employees and US Government Contractors: Difference in copyright
  • Publishing agreements for authors affiliated with US Government Contractors and US National Laboratories
  • NIH/CDC/FDA Employees
  • NASA Employees

Subscription publishing

Subscription publishing

Modified on: Fri, 27 May, 2022 at 1:48 PM

If you publish your article under a subscription model (also known as a ‘traditional model’ and ‘non-open access model’), your article will only be available to subscribers of the journal.

You will not be asked to pay article processing charge (APC), but please note that some of our journals may apply page and colour charges.

Please check Instructions for authors or Submission guidelines section on the journal’s website for more details.

Although the US government may not own rights due the public domain status of the material, a licence agreement must still be signed as part of the Next steps for publishing your article.

*Except for NIH/CDC/FDA and NASA employees who will need to make a non-standard request.

US Government Contractors (e.g. organisations running US national laboratories)

What are Creative Commons (CC) licenses?

What are Creative Commons (CC) licenses?

Modified on: Mon, 17 Nov, 2025 at 11:49 AM

Copyright is a legal right which protects the intellectual property in a creative work. It covers the right to copy or reproduce a work, to sell and distribute those copies to the public, and to adapt the work and create derivatives.

CC licenses offer an easy way to allow others to use a copyrighted work, and define how a work may be shared or reused. They are an important aspect of OA publishing and accelerating science.

Creative Commons licenses provide a standardized way to grant the public permissions to use their creative work under copyright law and describe permitted ways of such re-use. There are six different types of Open Access (OA) licences.

Springer Nature supports CC licensing and offers CC BY, CC BY NC and CC BY NC ND, but please note that the offer depends on the licence offered by the journal as well as other criteria, such as funder requirements.

For more information about OA licences at Springer Nature, please visit our copyright and licensing guide.

What are the differences between CC BY and CC BY NC ND licences?

What are the differences between CC BY and CC BY NC ND licences?

Modified on: Tue, 15 Oct, 2024 at 12:04 AM

  • CC BY - Creative Commons Attribution licence Readers can share and adapt your article for any purpose, including commercially, as long as the authors are credited.

  • CC BY-NC-ND - Creative Commons Attribution, Non-Commercial, No Derivatives licence Your article can be shared for non-commercial purposes as long as the authors are credited. Permission from Springer Nature is needed for commercial re-use or sharing adapted and derivative versions.

CC BY and CC BY-NC-ND licences provide permissions for sharing open access content.

Both CC BY and CC BY-NC-ND licences require that the original author and version is credited. There are differences in what users can do with your work depending on the licence you have published under.

CC BY

CC BY-NC-ND

Notes

Yes

Yes

Authors must be credited

Yes

Yes

Freely available to read and download

Yes

Only for non-commercial purposes

Share, distribute or republish the final published work

Yes

No

Sell or re-use the article for commercial purposes without permission

Yes

No

Share adapted or derivative versions without permission

Please note that the choice of license does not impact the Article Processing Charge (APC).

To learn more about OA licences visit our copyright and licensing guide.

Which CC licences are available to publish under?

Which CC licences are available to publish under?

Modified on: Tue, 15 Jul, 2025 at 8:26 PM

Open access (OA) articles in Springer Nature journals are published under Creative Commons licences. These provide an industry-standard framework to support re-use of OA material. Details of the OA licences offered to authors can be found on our individual journal websites, and more information about Creative Commons licence terms is available below and on the Creative Commons website.

Further information can be found on Springer Nature’s guide to licensing, copyright and author rights for journal articles.

Who grants permission to use NC-ND content in commercial or derivative contexts?

Who grants permission to use NC-ND content in commercial or derivative contexts?

Modified on: Tue, 16 Jul, 2024 at 10:37 AM

Authors choosing to publish under the CC BY-NC-ND licence grant Springer Nature exclusive commercial and derivative rights, which enable Springer Nature as the author’s trusted partner to make responsible use of this content, and to grant permissions for re-use where appropriate.

Authors choosing to publish under the CC BY-NC-ND licence grant Springer Nature exclusive commercial and derivative rights, which enables Springer Nature to protect the work against piracy and infringement of the CC BY-NC-ND re-use terms that the author has chosen. As a publisher we have an expert anti-piracy team that is well-equipped to proactively identify misuse and respond quickly to address these concerns. Without exclusive rights, Springer Nature would be unable to respond to cases of infringement and inappropriate use of content, and authors would have to pursue cases of infringement themselves.

Discovery partner Open Access lists and journals that have recently changed to Open Access

Discovery partner Open Access lists and journals that have recently changed to Open Access

Modified on: Wed, 13 Nov, 2024 at 1:20 PM

We regret that we are experiencing an issue with our Open Access autoholdings feeds in which a small number of journals contain some links out to paywalled content.

This may happen when a journal has recently swapped from subscription or Hybrid status into a fully Open Access status. When added to an OA list for discovery partners, all volumes of the journal will be included, instead of only the ones with fully OA content.

Unfortunately, we do not currently have a functionality in our systems to partition a journal’s metadata in a way that would only send over fully-OA volumes in these feeds rather than the entire run of the journal that will include paywalled content.

Go to the ExLibris knowledge base for the 360: Open Access in Springer Nature title lists page for additional information. We can confirm that work is being done on the Springer Nature side, but there is currently no timeline for a resolution.

We apologize for the inconvenience in the meantime and hope to have a resolution live as soon as possible.

Download MARC records for books or journals that were released since your last download

Download MARC records for books or journals that were released since your last download

Modified on: Mon, 10 Aug, 2026 at 8:23 AM

To update your library system with recently released titles, download new MARC records.

  • Follow the steps in “Download MARC records for newly licensed content”, or open the bookmarked link from your first download.
  • Under “Choose filters and format”, go to “Modified date and record status”.
    • Set “Select date (starting from)” to the date when you last downloaded records.
    • Select “Only new”
  • Check the text next to the Download button. If it says “We couldn’t find any titles”, check that you have selected the correct business models and copyright years. If your selections are correct, no titles have been added since your last download.
  • Download your MARC records. You can repeat these steps whenever you want to check for newly released titles.

Download MARC records for books or journals that were retracted since your last download

Download MARC records for books or journals that were retracted since your last download

Modified on: Mon, 10 Aug, 2026 at 8:35 AM

To remove retracted titles from your library system, download a list of deleted MARC records.

Get a list of “deleted” titles
  • Follow the steps in “Download MARC records for newly licensed content”, or open the bookmarked link from your first download.
  • Under “Choose filters and format”, go to “Modified date and record status”.
  • Set “Select date (starting from)” to the date when you last downloaded deleted records.
  • Select “Only deleted”.
  • Under “Choose filters and format”, go to “Format”.
  • Select “Excel title list”.
  • Check the text next to the Download button. If it says “We couldn’t find any titles”, check that you have selected the correct business models and copyright years. If your selections are correct, no titles have been deleted since your last download.
  • Download your Excel title list.
Check if you should remove the “deleted” titles

For each title in the list:

  • Open the “DOI URL”.
  • Check whether your library still has access to the full book, or to paywalled chapters in the book.
  • If your library still has access, keep the title in your library system.
  • If your library no longer has access, or the DOI URL leads to a 404 page, remove the title from your library system. You can repeat these steps whenever you want to check for deleted titles.
Why can I still access some “deleted” titles?

Bibliographic records are marked as deleted in two situations:

  • The book has been retracted. The book is no longer available to anyone on Springer Nature Link.
  • The book can no longer be newly licensed. This happens when Springer Nature no longer has the right to license the book to libraries. The book may still be available on Springer Nature Link. Organisations that licensed it in the past may still have access.

Download MARC records for books or journals where the bib records changed since your last download

Modified on: Mon, 10 Aug, 2026 at 8:30 AM

To update bibliographic records in your library system with the most recent metadata, download changed MARC records.

  • Under “Choose filters and format”, go to “Modified date and record status”.
    • Set “Select date (starting from)” to the date when you last downloaded records.
    • Select “Only modified”

Download MARC records for newly licensed content

Download MARC records for newly licensed content

Modified on: Mon, 10 Aug, 2026 at 8:25 AM

Get MARC records for your library’s newly licensed Journal or eBook collections, packages, or single titles. You can use these records to add the content to your library catalogue and other systems.

Understand what you have licensed

Before you start, check your licence agreement, invoice, or discussion with your Licensing Manager. Confirm which collections, packages, or single titles you have licensed, and which copyright years are included. You may also find it useful to read: Understanding Springer Nature eBook licensing models.

Select and download MARC records
  • Go to the MARC page in the Librarian Portal and select Books or Journals.
  • Find and select the business model you have licensed.
  • Under “Choose filters and format”, select the copyright years you have licensed.
  • Under “Modified date and record status”, select “Custom dates”. Enter the start date of your contract.
  • Check the text next to the Download button. If it says “We couldn’t find any titles”, check that you have selected the correct business models and copyright years.
  • Download your MARC records. You can also click Bookmark and save the generated link. This lets you quickly download updated records later, including records for titles that are published after your first download.
Why enter your contract start date?

This helps you avoid downloading records for journals and books that are not part of your entitlement. For example, a book may have been included in a business model for a copyright year, but later been removed because Springer Nature’s agreement with a third-party publisher ended.

In that case, we can no longer license the book. Your library has not paid a licensing fee for these books. Internally, they are sometimes referred to as “Springer Nature Link Hosting Only” (SLHO).

Download MARC records for your entire eBook or journal holdings

Download MARC records for your entire eBook or journal holdings

Modified on: Mon, 10 Aug, 2026 at 8:40 AM

In rare cases, you may want to add bibliographic records for all your licensed content to your library system. There are two ways to get the full set of records.

Download MARC records for each contract

Use this option if you can identify the collections, packages, or single titles, and the copyright years included in each contract.

  • Find the licence agreement or invoice for each contract with Springer Nature. Contact your Licensing Manager if you need help identifying what you have licensed.
  • For each contract, follow the steps in “Download MARC records for newly licensed content”, or open the bookmarked link from your first download.
Download MARC records for your full holdings

Use this option if you want to start from your current holdings instead.

  • Go to the Holdings page in the Librarian Portal.
  • Download the holdings report for “Available” or “Licensed” journals or eBooks.
  • Open the report in Excel as a TSV file.
  • Go to the MARC page in the Librarian Portal.
  • Select Books or Journals.
  • Select Single titles.
  • Copy and paste ISBNs or ISSNs from the holdings report into the “Enter ISBNs/ISSNs to include” text field. Use batches of no more than 15,000 titles.
  • Download your MARC records.

KBART and MARC records via API

KBART and MARC records via API

Modified on: Thu, 26 Sep, 2024 at 12:18 PM

MARC records via API

You can get all book MARC records from the Metadata Downloader via a public API.

Format:

metadata.springernature.com/api/metadata?eisbn=&format=MARCxml

Example:

metadata.springernature.com

The eISBN attribute is sometimes called Online ISBN. We only support MARC XML records, not MARC21 records.

KBART metadata via API

We offer KBART Automation, via which your holdings can automatically be updated from our servers. Information is available in the Librarian Portal.

MARC records in the Librarian Portal “Date modified and record status”

MARC records in the Librarian Portal “Date modified and record status”

Modified on: Fri, 4 Oct, 2024 at 4:07 PM

Record statuses

The statuses are about the bibliographic record, not about the book itself. So for example Modified does not mean that the book content has been edited, but that the metadata about the book has changed.

Bib records are Added on or around the day of publication for books and on or around the day of publication of the first article for journals.

Bib records are Modified when important changes are made to the metadata of the book or journal.

Bib records are marked as Deleted in two situations:

  • If the book is Retracted. The book will no longer be available to anyone on SpringerLink.
  • If the book Can not be newly licensed. This happens when we no longer have the right to license the book to libraries. The book will still be available on SpringerLink, and can be accessed by organisations who licensed it in the past.
Workflow examples
Add recently published books to your library system, or update book metadata

For eBook collections, reference modules, and Access & Select, new books are continuously published in the current and any future copyright year. Therefore, you may want to add titles to your catalogue or discovery system from time to time. Also, changes in metadata might be useful to transfer to your system.

  • Find, in your own notes, the most recent date you added / updated books from us.
  • Select the collections or other content you have licensed, as well as the licensed copyright years.
  • Under ‘Date modified and record status’ select ‘Starting from’ and choose the date you last added books. Also choose ‘New and modified’.
  • Make any other filter and format changes you need, and download your records.
Remove retracted books from your library system
  • Find, in your own notes, the most recent date you removed books from us.
  • Select the collections or other content you have licensed, as well as the licensed copyright years.
  • Under ‘Date modified and record status’ select ‘Starting from’ and choose the date you last removed books. Also choose ‘Only deleted’.
  • Choose Excel title list as format.
  • Make any other filter and format changes you need, and download your Excel file.
  • Open the Excel file and open each DOI URL in a browser.
    • If the book details are shown, then the book can not be newly licensed, which only impacts new license agreements. Since you have already licensed the title, you should still have access and the book should not be deleted from your system.
    • If the page shows ‘Page not found’ or ‘DOI not available’, or similar, the book has been Retracted and you can delete it from your system.

MARC records via FTP

MARC records via FTP

Modified on: Thu, 26 Sep, 2024 at 10:52 AM

We can deliver MARC records (not OCLC MARC records) based on most licensing models, including subject collections and Access & Select. To get set up with FTP delivery of MARC records, email our MARC team and include the following information.

  • Which Springer Nature collections you have purchased, including copyright year(s), e.g. Math and Statistics CRY 2017
  • The frequency of updates you would like (the standard is monthly but shorter intervals are possible)
  • By default, uploads will be made to a Springer Nature FTP site for you to download the data, with your own login credentials. If your processes require uploads to your own FTP instead, provide the address, credentials to access and the folder to use. Make sure we have read/write access. We will confirm via email when you can start to use the FTP service, which will remain in effect indefinitely.

New titles coming to KBART files

New titles coming to KBART files

Modified on: Mon, 14 Apr, 2025 at 10:25 AM

If you are using the KBART files of “Licensed/Available Journals” or “Licensed/Available eBooks” retrieved from the Holdings (KBART) section of the Librarian Portal, newly-launched Springer Nature titles will appear within a week. If your institution has set up KBART automation, they will appear in your discovery service a further 1-2 weeks after appearing on the Librarian Portal files.

If you are using the public KBART title lists page, or use a discovery service without KBART automation and manually select collections within the discovery service, new launches will appear after a month.

Springer Nature MARC Record Enhancements

Springer Nature MARC Record Enhancements

Modified on: Fri, 29 May, 2026 at 10:24 AM

Release Notes July 2025

This release outlines updates to Springer Nature MARC records, supporting two key objectives:

  • Adding accessibility metadata to support the European Accessibility Act (EAA)
  • Improving how Living Reference Works are identified by linking new eBook packages to library codes
1. Accessibility Metadata Enhancements

Springer Nature MARC records now include standardized metadata describing the accessibility features of digital content, i.e. Springer Nature eBooks. These enhancements support compliance with the European Accessibility Act (EAA) and improve discovery of accessible resources for libraries and users.

New MARC Fields Introduced

Field

Field description

Indicators

Subfields

341

Accessibility features

(technical or structural)

1st: 0

2nd: #

$b = value

$2 = source

532

Accessibility notes

1st: 8

2nd: #

$a = value or summary (free text)

Example MARC XML Snippets

Short alternative textual descriptions

onix

Publisher contact for further accessibility information: accessibilitysupport@springernature.com

Implementation Notes
  • These fields follow MARC21 standards and are populated according to current metadata specifications.
  • The MARC format for accessibility is still evolving, and ongoing discussions in the library community may lead to updates in future MARC releases.
  • These updates focus on accessibility features for the PDF format, which is the primary format described in Springer Nature MARC records.
  • On the Springer Nature Link platform, users will see additional accessibility details for other formats, such as ePub.
2. Improved Identification of Living Reference Works

To enhance cataloguing accuracy and discovery of Living Reference Works, new MARC mappings now link eBook packages to standardized library codes.

With the current MARC release, a total of 30 new library codes have been registered at the German ISIL and Library Code Agency at Berlin State Library. These codes are included in MARC records and facilitate better integration with library systems.

3. Following the RDA content standard

The MARC Records from Springer Nature are following the RDA standard, helping libraries to get modern and content focused metadata for their resources. As per the standard, the following three fields have been added to our MARC-records:

Field

Field description

Indicators

Subfields

336

Content type

1st: #

2nd: #

$a = text

$b = txt

$2 = rdacontent

337

Media type

1st: #

2nd: #

$a = computer

$b = c

$2 = rdamedia

338

Carrier type

1st: #

2nd: #

$a = online resource

$b = cr

$2 = rdacarrier

4. Subject assignment

In our MARC data we include metadata to explain the subject matter of our contents. Primarily, we describe the subjects in the fields:

  • 50 (Library of Congress Call Number)
  • 72 (Subject Category Code)
  • 82 (Dewey Decimal Classification Number) These are three different subject classifications with different taxonomies. When we are filling out this data, we are mapping each separate subject scheme to our own subject taxonomy. Since different subject taxonomies are involved, made with different purposes and with different level of ‘depth’ or nuance per subject, there can be cases where the subjects displayed in these fields are very unspecific.
Summary of Changes
  • New accessibility metadata fields (341 and 532) support EAA compliance and enhance discoverability of accessible content.
  • 30 newly registered library codes now identify Living Reference Works across disciplines, improving catalog integration and user discovery.
Contact & Feedback

If you have questions about these updates or would like to provide feedback, please contact us via our webform.

We welcome input from the library and metadata communities as we continue to enhance our MARC records in line with global standards and evolving needs.

Cancellation process for the monthly subscription to Nature+

Cancellation process for the monthly subscription to Nature+

Modified on: Mon, 25 May, 2026 at 12:24 PM

We hope that you are pleased with your subscription, but if you decide it’s not right for you, you may cancel your next monthly renewal at any time.

Cancelling will prevent all further subscription charges. It will not entitle you to a refund for current or previous charges. You will continue to have access to your subscription until your current billing period ends.

You can manage your subscription by logging into your Springer Nature account and following these few steps:

We recommend cancelling your subscription at least 72 hours before the end of your current billing period.

  1. Once logged into your Springer Nature account, select Subscriptions and Purchases and click Nature+ digital subscriptions.

If prompted, select your Region and click Log in

then My Account

  1. Select Manage Subscriptions

  2. Click Cancel subscription

After selecting a cancellation reason, your Nature+ subscription will be displayed as inactive, with no upcoming orders to display.

You will continue to have access to Nature+ until the end of your current billing period.

Should you wish to reinstate your Nature+ subscription at a later date, you can re-subscribe on the Nature+ homepage.

If you are experiencing difficulty cancelling your subscription, you can contact our Customer Service team.

How are Nature+ subscription payments processed?

How are Nature+ subscription payments processed?

Modified on: Tue, 16 Dec, 2025 at 4:29 PM

Nature+ uses Shopify to manage all recurring subscription payments. These payments are securely processed by Stripe ensuring transactions comply with strict banking and data protection standards.

In some countries, banks may require a monthly payment mandate to be set up between Stripe and your account. If this applies to you, your bank will contact you directly to confirm the mandate.

If you are unsure whether this applies in your country, we recommend checking with your bank or payment provider.

Click Unlink

After a few moments, your account should be unlinked.

Enter a new email address and click Update email address to confirm these details.

IMPORTANT: To ensure this change filters through to your Nature+ and Shopify accounts, please go back to your Account page and select Subscriptions and purchases, then Nature+ digital subscriptions:

Select your Region and click Log in

This will unify your Nature+ account details.

If you would like further assistance, please contact our Customer Service team.

Why was an expired card charged for Renewal of my Nature+ subscription?

Why was an expired card charged for Renewal of my Nature+ subscription?

Modified on: Mon, 25 May, 2026 at 12:50 PM

Some banks and card networks offer a service called Card Updater. This service automatically updates your card details, such as expiration dates or replacement card numbers, so that recurring subscription payments continue without interruption.

Should you wish to opt out of this service you will need to contact your bank or card issuer directly to disable this feature.

If you no longer require your Nature+ subscription, it is important to cancel the subscription to prevent further charges.

Cancellation process for the monthly subscription to Nature+

If you would like further information or assistance, please contact our Customer Service team.

Nature.com

Individual research or study assistance

Individual research or study assistance

Modified on: Wed, 16 Jul, 2025 at 4:14 PM

Nature Portfolio is unable to provide direct assistance with your research. This is because we are a specialist publisher of scientific material, rather than a research group.

Information that you may find useful is available from Nature Portfolio, either in the printed edition or on the website.

Please feel free to explore Nature Portfolio index of products and services. Alternatively, use our website search facility to locate published content that may be of interest to you.

Listed below are just a few of the Nature Portfolio websites that you may find useful:

Nature Publishing Index - Ranks institutions according to the number of primary research articles published in Nature journals.

nature.com Podcasts - A podcast is like a radio show that you can download and listen to whenever you want.

nature.com Webcasts - Lectures, seminars, webinars and tutorials video streamed to participants in different locations.

Science-Business Exchange - SciBX provides a unique and essential scientific and commercial perspective on today’s breakthrough science.

Nature Conferences - Unique events that will become essential resources for the researchers of today and tomorrow.

Nature Events Directory - Resource for scientists looking for the latest conferences, courses, meetings and symposia.

Nature Careers - Recruit, Upload your CV (Resume), Apply For Jobs, Search Jobs, Advertise Vacancies, Jobs News.

Nature Scitable - Faculty: Free online genetics resources help your students grow into researchers.

Locate articles on nature.com using search

Locate articles on nature.com using search

Modified on: Tue, 1 Oct, 2024 at 1:55 PM

There are two levels of Search functionality on nature.com which are available to any user, although depending on the content you may need to be a subscriber or registered user to view the content of the search results.

Basic Search which can be accessed via the Search field or Search button at the top of most of the pages on the site. You can use the basic search to locate content using keywords, phrases, the article title, an author or the DOI (Digital Object Identifier).

There is also an Advanced Search which enables the user to search using multiple criteria, including all of the above as well as by specific journal(s) and date ranges. To search within specific journals, start to type in the name of the journal and a list of available titles will appear from which you can select.

The search results are displayed in order of relevance by default, but this sort can be changed to display in date order by selecting the relevant option by clicking on the arrow next to “Sort by Relevance”.

You can narrow down the results using filters by clicking on the arrows next to the Article Type, Journal and Date options at the top of the results.

Please note that our online archives are not exhaustive and you may find that we do not hold the required article, particularly if it is very old. For such articles we suggest using PubMed, which has an extensive archive of our journals, or utilising the British Library’s document supply service.

Also, please visit our subject areas to find your subject of interest.

Nature Portfolio and reference manager support

Nature Portfolio and reference manager support

Modified on: Tue, 9 Jul, 2024 at 10:30 AM

Article citations on nature.com are available in ‘RIS’ format and can be imported directly to your reference management software. We do hope to provide support for additional file formats in future.

To download: click the ‘Citation’ button or ‘Download Citation’ link located either towards the top or to the right side of the article abstract or full text page (see examples below)

Unfortunately we are unable to assist with third party software issues. If you require assistance with your reference management tools you should contact the company which owns the software you are using.

Nature Research Archive

Nature Research Archive

Modified on: Mon, 17 Mar, 2025 at 11:25 AM

The entire Nature archive dating back to Volume 1, Issue 1 (November 1869) is published online with restricted access.

A personal subscription to Nature includes access to the archive from January 1997 to current day. Access to the archive material prior to 1997 is available via an Institutional Site Licence or alternatively individual articles can be purchased online.

The Nature+ subscription packages offer access to 55 of our Nature Portfolio titles from the year 2017. Please see Which journals are included within the Nature+ subscription? and our Nature+ FAQ’s for more information.

If you belong to an institution; you may recommend the archives to your librarian and ask them to complete the online form found on the Springer Nature Librarian pages.

All of the available supplementary information for the entire archive has been posted on the website. If the supplementary information that you require is not currently available, then it is unlikely to be recovered.

Other resources may provide further information. A citation service called PubMed, which has an extensive archive of our journal abstracts, or alternatively The British Library.

Google Scholar may be a useful tool for locating articles and citations that are published or archived online.

Natureevents

Natureevents

Modified on: Sat, 12 Oct, 2024 at 4:17 AM

The Natureevents directory is an online based listings service for the scientific community and covers all events, conferences & announcements with a scientific focus.

It is organised along disciplinary guidelines and each page contains an internal search engine covering date, event title, location, keyword. Use of this tool will provide a full list of events within the specified guidelines.

For a more detailed perspective, simply click on the title of the event and this will open a window showing all the supplied information regarding that event. This should include date, title, location, an overview of the event, audience and aims, full contact details, booking deadlines, organisers, speakers and presenters and, should you be unable to attend, details on how to obtain proceedings papers and notes of the event.

Delivery time in Japan

Delivery time in Japan

Modified on: Thu, 14 Nov, 2024 at 1:30 PM

Weekly magazine Nature is delivered by Japan Post’s “Yu-mail” service.

From October 2021, due to the revision of the Postal Law, Saturday delivery of postal items will be suspended and the delivery time will gradually be moved back by about a day. There is a possibility that the delivery of Nature will be delayed by a few days. We appreciate your understanding.

For more information, please visit the Japan Post website

Nature titled journals (monthly) are dispatched as soon as they imported from overseas.

Currently the following journals are experiencing major delays in production in the U.S. and transportation to Japan. We appreciate your patience.

  • Nature Biotechnology
  • Nature Genetics
  • Nature Structural & Molecular Biology
  • Nature Medicine
  • Nature Neuroscience
  • Nature Immunology
  • Nature Methods
  • Nature Protocols

-In the case of direct debit : the receipt will be substituted with the entry in the bankbook.

After the journal is published, the average shipping time for Offprints and Posters are:

  • 35 business days for deliveries in Europe
  • 50 business days for deliveries in the rest of the world. We are currently not selling offprints and posters to customers based in China. Unfortunately, there is no time frame as to when this changes but we expect this to be a long term situation.

We are currently not selling offprints and posters to customers based in China. Unfortunately, there is no time frame as to when this changes but we expect this to be a long term situation.

APC invoice tax exemption for international organisations in the EU

APC invoice tax exemption for international organisations in the EU

Modified on: Thu, 7 Nov, 2024 at 11:02 AM

Certain diplomatic and international entities in the EU may be exempt from VAT for specific goods and services under international law, if accredited or recognized by their host EU country. There are limits and conditions to the exemption and additional formal requirements must be followed.

We will need to process the tax removal manually. Please go through the checkout section during the Next steps for publishing your article and either pay online or request an offline invoice. You may then send us the invoice and the required documentation (detailed below) in order for us to process an invoice correction to remove the taxes if approved.

In order for us to consider removing the VAT from your APC invoice, it is mandatory to fill out the EU-provided form completely and have it stamped and signed by the competent authority. We would ask you to use the PDF version of the form.

Afterwards, please submit the form via Contact Publishing Support (referring to the concerned invoice number). We will then get back as soon as possible. If approved and if you have paid online, we will then refund the amount overpaid. If you select an offline invoice, an admin fee of 75 USD/55 EUR/50 GBP is charged for most journals.

APC invoice tax exemption for Tax-Exempt Organisations in the USA

APC invoice tax exemption for Tax-Exempt Organisations in the USA

Modified on: Thu, 14 Nov, 2024 at 10:53 AM

Springer Nature understands that tax-exempt organizations and institutions in the USA may need VAT removed from their APC invoices. To request VAT removal:

  • Request an invoice or pay online during Next steps for publishing your article (after acceptance). If you select an offline invoice, an admin fee of 75 USD/55 EUR/50 GBP is charged for most journals.
  • Send us your invoice and tax exemption certificate through Contact Publishing Support.
  • If approved, our team will manually process an invoice correction to remove the tax. If you have paid online, we will then refund the amount overpaid.

Deposition of published Open Access articles on PubMed

Deposition of published Open Access articles on PubMed

Modified on: Tue, 15 Jul, 2025 at 8:09 PM

Springer Nature automatically deposits open access articles in PubMed Central (PMC) and Europe PubMed Central (EPMC) on publication under the following conditions:

Conditions
Articles in fully OA journals with full PMC deposition agreement

For authors publishing in our fully OA journals, where the journal has a full PMC deposition agreement we will automatically deposit all articles to PubMed Central. You may check the PMC agreement type in the PMC journal list.

If the journal does not yet have an agreement, or is out of subject scope, we will deposit articles that acknowledge funding from a PMC partner (e. g. NIH) or EPMC funder, or a funder approved by PubMed Central for deposit in compliance with a public or open access policy.

To enable us to identify an OA article as eligible for deposit, authors must provide details of the relevant research funder, grant number and recipient name in the structured funding fields provided during the submission process.

Articles in hybrid and Transformative Journals

As of 2019, Open Access articles published in hybrid and transformative journals are eligible to be deposited in PubMed Central if

  • the journal of publication is currently indexed in MEDLINE. or if

  • the article acknowledges funding from a PMC partner (e. g. NIH) or EPMC funder, or a funder approved by PubMed Central for deposit in compliance with a public or open access policy. To enable us to identify an OA article as eligible for deposit, authors must provide details of the relevant research funder, grant number and recipient name in the structured funding fields provided during the submission process.

Deposition timeline and funder compliance
  • The estimated time for articles to appear on their platform is six weeks.
  • Online first articles are not deposited until assigned to an issue.
  • For more information about a deposition timeline and funder compliance, please check ‘Timeline and funder compliance’ under Self-archiving, manuscript deposition, and digital preservation.

What is an Article in Press?

What is an Article in Press?

Modified on: Wed, 5 Aug, 2026 at 4:05 PM

  • What is an Article in Press?
  • How is the Article in Press different to the Version of Record?
  • How do I cite the Article in Press?
  • How does indexing work with an Article in Press?
  • Can corrections/ amendments be made to the Article in Press?
  • Why does my Article in Press have a watermark over the body text / figures?
  • Can I publicise and promote my work once the Article in Press is published?
  • Can I share the Article in Press version to repositories and scholarly sharing networks?
  • Is it OK to have a pre-print and an Article in Press online?
  • When will my Article in Press be online?
  • How will I know when the full version of my paper is published?
  • Can I opt-out of this service?
  • Can my Article in Press be taken down?
  • What do I need to do if my manuscript has scheduling needs? I.e. for press release, ARPD, embargo, or funding alignment.
  • What happens for manuscripts with co-publications?
  • What do the dates on the article mean?
What is an Article in Press?

An ‘Article in Press’ is an early access version of an accepted, peer-reviewed manuscript that has a permanent Digital Object Identifier(DOI) and can be viewed, shared, and cited.

An Article in Press is provided to accelerate access to findings while final edits, checks, and layout are being completed. The manuscript may be subject to further editing, as errors affecting the content maybe discovered during the final production process.

How is the Article in Press different to the Version of Record?

Article in Press versions still need final edits, checks, and layout, and do not have the full functionality of the Version of Record. For example, supplementary files may still need to be added, links to references may not resolve yet, etc.

Errors in the Article in Press version will be corrected during the author proofing stages of production. Corrections will be reflected in the final Version of Record.

An Article in Press is clearly labelled online to show that this is not the final Version of Record. All legal disclaimers relating to the journal apply.

When the final Version of Record is published, it will replace the Article in Press, retaining the same DOI.

How do I cite the Article in Press?

Once the final article is assigned to a specific volume and issue of the journal, the “article in press” version will be replaced with the completed, published version. It will then appear within the corresponding volume and issue. The original online publication date—the date the article first became available and recorded as ‘published date’ —will remain unchanged.

Please note that although articles in press do not yet contain full bibliographic details, they may still be cited. Use the first online publication year and DOI when referencing them, and include the information on author(s), article title, publication year, and DOI in your citation.

How does indexing work with an Article in Press?

The Article in Press will not be indexed in any indexing services. When the final, fully typeset version of your article is published, it will be automatically submitted to PubMed for indexing.

Can corrections/ amendments be made to the Article in Press?

While you cannot make amendments or corrections to the Article in Press, further copy editing, formatting, and quality checks will take place before the Version of Record is published.

If you notice any errors or editing issues in the Article in Press then please mark these in your proof link. If you have already reviewed your proofs and did not correct these errors in the proof or if you would like to amend the Creative Commons licence your publication is under, please submit a ticket. We will notify the production team accordingly.

Why does my Article in Press have a watermark over the body text / figures?

The watermark on the Article in Press version is included intentionally. It appears on each page to make it clear to readers that this is the Article in Press and not the final, fully typeset version of your article. This helps to prevent the Article in Press from being mistaken for the version of record, as further copyediting and production work will still take place before final publication.

Can I publicise and promote my work once the Article in Press is published?

Yes, the Article in Press provides early access to your accepted findings to help accelerate the discovery and impact of your research. Your Article in Press has a permanent Digital Object Identifier and is available to be read, cited and shared.

Journalists are welcome to report on the findings of your study as it is no longer under embargo.

You are also welcome to send out your own materials promoting your research.

When the final Version of Record is published, it will replace the Article in Press, retaining the same DOI.

Can I share the Article in Press version to repositories and scholarly sharing networks?

The Article in Press is made available under the same terms as the published Version of Record. This means that for OA articles, authors can choose to share the Article in Press version on to other locations permitted by the terms of the CC licence.

For authors who have chosen CC BY this includes any location; for authors who have opted for the CC BY-NC-ND licence this includes all non-commercial locations, such as institutional and funder repositories. However, since the Article in Press will be shortly replaced by the published version of record, we would recommend that authors share links to the Article in Press version and then post the final version of record to their chosen repository once available.

Where authors have opted for the subscription publication route, they can share their accepted manuscript (AM) after the relevant embargo period has elapsed. Self-archiving of the Version of Record or Article in Press is not permitted for subscription articles.

Is it OK to have a pre-print and an Article in Press online?

The Article in Press will be online in the same capacity as the version of record (which will replace it once ready) and will therefore have the same licensing. There would be no issues with the Article in Press being present online at the same time as a pre-print.

When will my Article in Press be online?

Please allow 1-2 weeks after completing your production proof & licensing agreement for your Article in Press to go online.

How will I know when the full version of my paper is published?

You will be notified by email upon publication of your version of record.

Can I opt-out of this service?

Articles in press are in place for Fully OA journals as an author benefit to accelerate the dissemination of research and is embedded in the journal workflow. Authors will not be able to opt out for individual articles.

Can my Article in Press be taken down?

We are not able to take down an AiP once it has been published. This version serves as the official early publication of the article and is part of the standard scholarly record. However, on publication of the official version of the paper, the content on our website, and deposited with PubMed and third parties will be overwritten with the finalised content that has undergone typesetting and proofing.

What do I need to do if my manuscript has scheduling needs? I.e. for press release, ARPD, embargo, or funding alignment.

You will need to inform your editor as far in advance as possible if your manuscript requires a press release or specific publication date for any other reason. Once the Article in Press is published, we will not be able to schedule publications.

What happens for manuscripts with co-publications?

Please ensure these are handled in the same way as manuscripts with scheduling requirements (above).

What do the dates on the article mean?

Received Date: This is the date the article was first submitted to the journal.

Accepted Date: This is the date the article was accepted for publication in the journal.

Published Date: This is the date the article first appeared online after being accepted.

Version of Record Date: This is the date the final version of the article was published.

If there is an early version for sharing (eg an Article in Press) then the Published date will reflect the date the early version first published. If there is no early version publication then the Published date and Version of Record date will be the same.

Payment, renewals & Taxes

Cancel a paid subscription to any Nature Portfolio journal

Cancel a paid subscription to any Nature Portfolio journal

Modified on: Wed, 1 Apr, 2026 at 3:03 PM

Cancelling an annual subscription to any Nature Portfolio journal: Orders are regarded as firm and after the first issue of the volume has been dispatched subscription payments are not refundable. Taking out an annual subscription commits you for a minimum of one year.

And, in most cases no cancellations or refund requests for a journal will be accepted from the current year 14 days after a subscription has been entered and payment has been processed.

When purchasing a subscription which is sold as a ‘print plus on-line’ option, it is not possible to cancel or suppress either the print or the on-line part of the subscription, as it is sold as a package.

If you would like to cancel a paid Nature Portfolio paid subscription, please complete the details on our cancel a Nature Portfolio journal subscription form so we can process your request. Completing the cancel request form will raise a Customer Service ticket and the team will use that ticket to provide you with updates on your request on the email address you provided.

If you have a complimentary / free print subscription, please refer to the information found on our Cancel Complimentary Subscription page.

If you require further assistance, please contact our Customer Service team Please provide your Customer ID, your name and delivery address.

Cancel complimentary / free print subscription

Cancel complimentary / free print subscription

Modified on: Tue, 24 Sep, 2024 at 10:43 AM

If you would like to cancel your complimentary / free print subscription to any journal, please contact our Customer Service team. If possible, please provide a scan or photo of the latest shipping label to help us trace the subscription record when you send your request to us. If you are unable to include a copy of the shipping label with your request, please provide us with your full name and address details as they appear on the label.

Foreign Transaction / Purchase Fee

Foreign Transaction / Purchase Fee

Modified on: Thu, 15 Sep, 2022 at 5:02 PM

If you use a debit or credit card to pay for Springer Nature subscriptions you may be charged a Foreign Purchase Fee by your bank or card issuer. This is a charge levied by your financial institution and not by Springer Nature.

We advise you to contact your bank or card issuer if you have concerns or questions regarding any Foreign Purchase Fee as different countries and banks impose different fee charges depending on the location of the supplier (Springer Nature) and the currency of payment.

We process payments in US Dollars for customers in the Americas, Euros for customers in ROW and GBP for UK customers.

Important: For security reasons credit and debit card numbers cannot be accepted via email. Please help us to protect your information by not submitting payment in this manner.

Payment options available on Nature.com

Payment options available on Nature.com

Modified on: Thu, 27 Mar, 2025 at 1:00 PM

Payment options available on Nature.com

For items purchased from the web shop directly, we accept MasterCard, Visa, American Express, PayPal or Visa debit card. The currency in which your order is billed depends on the shipping address:

  • Customers from Canada, North and Latin America will be billed in US Dollars (USD).
  • Customers from Great Britain are billed in Pounds Sterling (GBP).
  • Customers from Europe, Africa, Asia/Pacific will be billed in Euros (EUR).
  • Customers from Japan will be billed in Japanese Yen (JPY). If you pay with a credit card or PayPal and your local currency is not one of those listed above the amount will be converted by your bank or credit card issuer automatically. This may incur foreign transaction fees from your banking provider.

Additional security services such as ‘Verified by Visa’ or ‘MasterCard Secure Code’ - may be request ed by your credit card provider, please contact them directly if required.

Other payment options

If you would like to pay via bank or wire transfer using a Proforma Invoice or Invoice, please contact us to request. Once we have created the order, you will need to add the invoice / proforma number as a reference on the transfer details for our Accounting Department to allocate your payment to your order.

US: Prepayment is required for all Springer Nature journal orders. You can pay using check or money order drawn on a US Bank. Customers from Canada paying by cheque for an order under $500 USD must have the cheque drawn from a US Bank.

ROW: Prepayment is required for all Springer Nature journal orders except for customers based in Germany, Austria and Switzerland where we allow invoicing via direct debit on request.

For Security Reasons, Credit Card Details Must Not Be Sent To Us By Email.

Auto payments for annual subscription renewals

The Automatic Payment option allows a subscriber to register their payment card at the time of purchasing from the web shop, allowing future renewals to be charged to the same card. These can only be applied at the time a subscription is purchased on Nature.com or Springerlink.com.

During the payment process of placing an order as you enter your card details, you will see an option box and you can select to opt into the auto payment option. Once the opt in box is selected, the card will be registered for any subsequent renewals to that Journal only. If you are purchasing multiple titles, each journal would need to be registered for automatic payments individually.

Please do not send your card details directly to customer service as we are unable to store them or add them to a subscription later. All card details submitted through the form are held on an encrypted Worldpay system.

2 weeks prior to the end of the current subscription we will notify you that the contract is due to expire soon and remind you that the auto renewal option was selected at the time of purchase. Then, 1 week prior to the expiry date we will attempt to take the payment.

If we are unable to initiate a payment attempt for any reason i.e. the card has expired, we will notify you via email with a new payment link where you can amend the card details. If the 1st attempt to collect payment is not successful, we will try 2 more times then if the subsequent requests fail, we will then send you a new payment link (active for 7 days) and request the card details are updated.

After the 3rd attempt, if we have been unable to charge the registered card and no new card information has been provided then we will suspend the subscription.

You can cancel the auto payment for renewals at any time by contacting our Customer Service team.

VAT/GST/ Sales Tax rules for Springer Nature’s print and digital products and Article Processing Charges (APC)

VAT/GST/ Sales Tax rules for Springer Nature’s print and digital products and Article Processing Charges (APC)

Modified on: Thu, 5 Mar, 2026 at 3:39 PM

This page provides information on the applicable VAT, GST and sales tax treatment for Springer Nature print and digital products, as well as Article Processing Charges (APCs).

General - Invoicing entity

Invoices to customers located outside the Americas are issued by Springer Nature Customer Service Center GmbH, Heidelberg, Germany. Customers located in the Americas receive their invoices from Springer Nature Customer Service Center LLC, New York, USA.

United Kingdom (UK)

All prices are exclusive of VAT.

Books and journals in printed and digital formats (and bundles thereof) are subject to VAT at the zero rate. Other digital products and Article Processing Charges (APCs) for open access publications are subject to VAT at the UK standard rate of 20%. UK VAT-registered customers should provide their full and valid VAT registration number. Where applicable, the UK reverse charge mechanism will be applied.

European Union (EU)

All prices are exclusive of VAT.

Books and journals in printed and digital formats (and bundles thereof) are subject to VAT at the reduced rate applicable in the EU Member State where the customer is located.

Other digital products and Article Processing Charges (APCs) for open access publications are subject to the standard VAT rate in customer`s country (e.g. Germany 19%, Austria 20%, etc).

EU VAT-registered customers should provide their full and valid VAT registration number, including country code. Where applicable, this enables:

  • VAT-exempt intra-Community supplies for print products, and
  • Application of the EU reverse charge mechanism for digital products and APCs.
United States of America (USA)

All prices are exclusive of Sales Tax.

Applicable US sales taxes will be charged in accordance with state and local regulations.

Canada

All prices are exclusive of GST/HST/QST.

GST/HST/QST will be charged at applicable rates and may apply to print and digital products and APCs.

Rest of World and South America

Prices are exclusive of VAT, GST, or any other indirect taxes.

Springer Nature Customer Service Center GmbH and/or LLC is VAT-registered in various non-EU and South American jurisdictions. In accordance with local legislation, VAT or GST will be charged on digital products and APCs supplied to customers located in these countries:

Region/Country

Region/Country

Region/Country

Region/Country

Australia

India

New Zealand

South Korea

Bahrain

Indonesia

Norway

Taiwan

Chile

Japan

Philippines

Thailand

Colombia

Saudi Arabia

Türkiye

Ghana

Switzerland

Singapore

United Arab Emirates

Iceland

Malaysia

South Africa

Article Processing Charges (APCs) are generally subject to the standard VAT/GST rate in these jurisdictions.

In many of these countries, the reverse charge mechanism may apply where the customer is a VAT/GST-registered business and provides a valid local VAT/GST registration number.

Sales to customers located in non-EU and South American countries not listed above are currently not subject to VAT or GST. Please note that this position may change. Globally, there is an increasing number of countries introducing indirect tax registration requirements for the supply of digital services, which we continue to monitor.

Further assistance

If you require further assistance, please contact our Customer Service team.

How to be considered as a peer reviewer at Springer Nature

How to be considered as a peer reviewer at Springer Nature

Modified on: Tue, 28 Jul, 2026 at 7:35 PM

If you are interested in peer reviewing at Springer Nature, we encourage you to apply for a Reviewing Editor role within the Springer Nature Reviewer Programme.

The Springer Nature Reviewer Programme brings together a select group of expert peer reviewers, offering them training, tailored support, and recognition. As a Reviewing Editor, you will join a distinguished community and gain access to a range of benefits, such as:

  • Public recognition for exceptional work on Springer Nature pages
  • On-demand certificates recognizing your work in the programme
  • Access to exclusive events, bespoke training courses, and online platforms designed to support you in your role
  • Global networking opportunities with like-minded peers in your field To apply for this unique opportunity, please complete our Reviewing Editor Application form.

Please note: All applications are carefully reviewed, and due to the volume received, you may not receive an immediate response. We appreciate your patience and understanding.

Modified on: Fri, 22 Nov, 2024 at 2:16 PM

We have, however, seen take up of this service decline across Springer Nature journals and we are exploring other options and ways for the work undertaken by our peer reviewers to be recognised and rewarded.

Reviewers are still able to add their reviewer activity to their Clarivate researcher profile should they wish, but until further notice Springer Nature reviewers are no longer able to automatically log their peer review contributions with the RRS.

Post-Publication

Articles not indexed by Web of Science

Articles not indexed by Web of Science

Modified on: Thu, 21 Nov, 2024 at 2:03 PM

Beginning of September 30, 2019, Web of Science has started to index Online First articles published in Springer and Palgrave journals. This new policy is applicable for online First articles published from October 1, 2019.

If an article has been published as an Online First article prior to September 30, 2019 and not yet been assigned to a volume and issue of the journal, then it will not generally have been indexed by indexing services such as Web of Science (WoS).

The Online First article published before September 30, 2019 will be indexed once it is assigned to an issue and has a complete bibliographic citation including the volume, issue and page numbers.

Please generally allow up to six weeks upon publication, before your article becomes available on WoS.

If your eligible article has not been indexed on Web of Science within this time frame, please contact Clarivate directly, using their Data change & missing article form.
