Electronic offprint sharing

Electronic offprint sharing

Modified on: Thu, 21 Nov, 2024 at 2:07 PM

Authors have the right to disseminate their article’s eOffprint (as a printout or by email) to their co-authors and research colleagues (for personal use by such colleagues). It is not allowed to distribute the eOffprint via a webpage.

The eOffprint is for personal use only and shall not be self archived in electronic repositories. If you wish to self-archive your article, please use the accepted manuscript version for posting on your own website.

You may further deposit the accepted manuscript version in any repository, provided it is only made publicly available 12 months after official publication or later and provided acknowledgement is given to the original source of publication and a link is inserted to the published article on Springer’s website. The link must be accompanied by the following text: “The final publication is available at link.springer.com”.

If you want to share your published manuscript on social media we recommend to use the SharedIt functionality.

Embargo period for Open Access articles

Modified on: Tue, 15 Jul, 2025 at 7:58 PM

The 6/12 months embargo does not apply to open access papers.

To find out which embargo period applies to your subscription article, please check our Policies on Self-archiving of papers published via the subscription route.

Online access to my published article

Online access to my published article

Modified on: Thu, 17 Oct, 2024 at 2:39 PM

There are several options available so you can obtain online access to your published article.

Access through your Institution

If your institution has subscribed to the electronic journal, you will have immediate full text access. If your institution does not have a license for the journal, the fastest way to access your published article online is to use the pay-per-view option. To order by pay-per-view:

  • Find your article in Springer Nature Link
  • Click on the ‘Buy now’ button
  • If you are not currently logged in then you will be prompted to log into your Springer account.
  • You will be taken to our online store facility where you can purchase the article.
  • Once the transaction is completed you will be sent an email with details on how to download the article.
SharedIt

The Springer Nature SharedIt content-sharing initiative means that links to view-only, full-text subscription research articles can be posted anywhere - including on social media platforms, author websites and in institutional repositories - so researchers can share research with colleagues and general audiences.

Authors will receive their article’s SharedIt link. Both authors and readers can share research using this link. SharedIt has been designed to facilitate personal, non-commercial, online sharing of research articles - also on platforms such as ResearchGate or Academia.edu.

Post-publication book corrections

Post-publication book corrections

Modified on: Mon, 29 Dec, 2025 at 3:09 PM

If you notice an error in your published book (such as text, metadata, or figures), please contact your publishing editor or submit a ticket and one of our agents will get back to you.

Note: those with asterisks (*) are mandatory.

Please make sure to include the following information:

  • Title:
  • ISBN/DOI*:
  • Corresponding author name & email address:
  • Error Description*:
  • Justification for correction*: If your article requires multiple changes, please annotate a PDF version or word tracked changes of the relevant pages and outline where the errors are in the text, as well as what the correct text should say.

We will assess whether the correction requires an erratum/correction or an update in later editions.

Self-archive Nature Portfolio manuscripts

Self-archive Nature Portfolio manuscripts

Modified on: Wed, 25 Sep, 2024 at 6:54 PM

Nature Portfolio’s License to Publish permits authors to self-archive the accepted version of their manuscript in PubMed Central or other appropriate funding body’s archive; their institution’s repositories; and, if they wish, on their personal websites. In all cases, the manuscript can be made publicly accessible six months after publication.

Nature Portfolio has not required authors of original research articles to transfer copyright since 2002. Our policies are explained in detail on the License to Publish page.

Manuscript Deposition Service

Several funding bodies and institutions have mandates that require authors to self-archive manuscripts in publicly accessible archives. In July 2008, Nature Portfolio launched its Manuscript Deposition Service. This free service helps authors fulfil a number of funder and institutional mandates, by depositing authors’ accepted manuscripts to PubMed Central on their behalf.

Where research has been supported by the relevant funders, Nature Portfolio’s Manuscript Deposition Service is available to authors publishing original research articles in Nature, the Nature research titles and many of the society and academic journals published by Nature Portfolio.

Our Manuscript Deposition Service enables authors to meet the open access or public access policies of various participating funders, making it simple and free for researchers to comply.

SNCS Contact Form: Inclusive Name Change Policy

SNCS Contact Form: Inclusive Name Change Policy

Modified on: Tue, 2 Jun, 2026 at 3:22 PM

Authors who have changed their name for reasons such as gender transition, religious conversion and others may request for their name, pronouns, and other relevant biographical information to be amended on works published prior to the change. Authors can choose for this correction to happen silently, without notification of the name change in either the pdf or the html of the paper, or alternatively they may do so by a formal public Correction, which will be visible on both the pdf and html versions of the published manuscript.

  • If you are an author and would like to request a name change please complete the Inclusive Name Change form.
  • Or, if you are an approved institutional representative by the author and are entitled to request the change on their behalf, complete the Institutions: Request Inclusive Name Change Policy Authors form.
  • When filling in this form:
  • Please include all works published by Springer Nature (including journal articles, book chapters, conference proceedings published by Nature Portfolio, Springer, BMC, Palgrave, Apress, Adis, and other Springer Nature imprints) that you wish to amend.
  • You should only fill in the email field for each individual item if there is an email that needs to be amended ― that is, if it contains your previous name. The updated email is to an address that is not associated with any of your affiliations in the manuscript, the email should be deleted rather than amended. Institutional affiliations cannot be amended.
  • In addition to your name, please note any initials that appear in Acknowledgements, Author Contributions, Conflict of Interest statements or elsewhere in the work that need to be amended.
  • For each work, please let us know if there are also any separate supplementary information files that contain your name that need to be amended.
  • Please ensure that you update your details in any of our submissions systems (such as EJP, Editorial Manager, SNAPP, ScholarOne) or platforms (nature.com, bmc.com, springer.com, palgrave.com) that you may be registered with. We are not allowed to change any information held in these systems on behalf of authors. If you don’t have an account with any of these, you can ignore this.
  • In addition, book authors whose bank details have changed should contact the globalroyaltysupport@springernature.com to update these to ensure that any royalties due are correctly receive.
  • To ensure that future references to your work are cited with your correct name, we recommend that you let your co-authors know once the works have been amended. If you encounter any issues with the forms, please notify the Content team and submit your request to content.springerlink@springernature.com.

Track your article’s performance

Track your article’s performance

Modified on: Fri, 26 Jul, 2024 at 8:08 AM

As an author, you can track the performance of your published articles online via Your Publications (login required).

You can also access it via the starting page of your Springer Nature Profile where you will need to login and navigate to “Your Research”. Scrolling down, you will find the section called “Your research”, which is separated into two tabs “Submitted” and “Published”:

Depending on your selection, you will either see the statuses of your submitted manuscripts or already published articles. If your manuscript has been accepted but does not yet appear in the “Published” section, it may still be in the Next steps for publishing your article or in production. Preparation

Cover letter for your manuscript

Cover letter for your manuscript

Modified on: Fri, 27 Aug, 2021 at 12:59 PM

A cover letter can be used to help convey a work’s importance to the editors.

It should also be used to highlight any potential issues such as related manuscripts currently under consideration in any other Springer Nature publication, as well as indicating whether you have had any prior discussions with a Springer Nature editor about the work described in the manuscript.

Please use the cover letter to declare that the manuscript is not currently being considered for publication in any other journal and, if necessary, please include any reviewers you wish to recommend or exclude (including the reasons why).

Finally, the cover letter is a good place to include any other issues or anything you were unsure of, that you have encountered whilst submitting your manuscript.

How to publish? – Step by step

Finding out a journal’s scope (manuscript suitability)

Finding out a journal’s scope (manuscript suitability)

Modified on: Wed, 20 Nov, 2024 at 8:36 AM

Please review the aims and scope of the journal on the journal’s homepage as well as abstracts of recently published papers. Find the right journal for your manuscript will help narrow down the search to the most suitable journals.

If the paper is suitable for the journal, please submit using the Submit Manuscript button on the journal’s homepage. The paper will be submitted to the Editor in Chief for initial assessment. If the paper is found suitable, it will be sent out for peer review. If not, a transfer will be suggested where applicable. You will be notified shortly in any case.

Is there a word limit for Springer Nature journal articles?

Is there a word limit for Springer Nature journal articles?

Modified on: Wed, 20 Nov, 2024 at 8:39 AM

We do not have restraints on the size of manuscripts submitted to most of our Springer Nature journals unless otherwise required and specified in the Submission Guidelines or Instructions for Authors.

Manuscripts can include an unlimited number of figures, tables and additional files but the abstract of the manuscript should not exceed 350 words.

However, please remember that for the benefit of peer reviewers and readers articles should be as concise as possible.

For more information, please see the Submission Guidelines or Instructions for Authors by selecting your journal of choice from the lists below:

BMC

Palgrave Macmillan

Nature Research

Springer

For any additional questions, please contact the relevant submission contact of the journal directly. This is how you will Find publishing contacts for Springer Nature journals.

Which indexes will index my article?

Which indexes will index my article?

Modified on: Fri, 2 Aug, 2024 at 7:59 AM

Springer Nature journals index articles in various indexing services, including Google Scholar, PMC, Web of Science, and SCOPUS, depending on the journal.

To see if your selected journal supports the index in question, please follow these simple steps, depending on the journal imprint:

Springer journals

Visit the journal homepage and scroll all the way down. You will find a complete list of supported indexing services under “Abstracted and indexed in”.

BMC

Visit the journal homepage and click “About”. You will find a complete list of supported indexing services under “Indexing Services”.

Nature and Nature portfolio journals

Check About indexing/abstracting, where you will find a complete list.

If you have not yet decided which journal you would like to publish with, please check Find the right journal for your manuscript.

Please generally allow around 6 weeks upon publication for your article to be indexed.

Writing a journal manuscript

Modified on: Wed, 20 Nov, 2024 at 8:30 AM

Springer Nature understands how important it for researchers to publish their results for the scientific community to see, in order to get recognition for your achievements and exchange ideas.

We are pleased you are considering our journals for this purpose. To help you write the best article possible we have designed a short series of free tutorials which will provide you with important points to consider, from your background reading and study design, to manuscript structuring and figure preparation. Please note that our course offerings are subject to change, however, some examples include Writing in English - Writing a manuscript - Introduction to Research Integrity - How to submit a journal article.

Publishing Costs

Costs of publishing in Springer Nature journal

Costs of publishing in Springer Nature journal

Modified on: Mon, 21 Jul, 2025 at 7:57 AM

The cost of publishing an article depends on the publishing model(s) offered by the journal.

If you wish to publish in one of our subscription based journals then in the majority of cases there is no charge. Charges may apply for colour figures or over-length articles and some journals have page charges. You can find information regarding all of these costs on the individual journal home pages.

If you wish to publish in an open access journal or an open access article in a hybrid journal, there will be an Article Processing Charge (APC) to be paid by the author or their funding institution. APC are listed on individual journal pages, either in Fees and Funding section on within Submission guidelines.

Visit our Open Access Books and Journals page for additional details about open access publishing.

If you are not sure which journal to publish your article in, visit our find the right journal to submit your article page for additional details.

We also provide journal author tutorials with interactive courses on our journal author academy page.

Fees for publishing in an “Open Choice” Journal

Fees for publishing in an “Open Choice” Journal

Modified on: Tue, 15 Jul, 2025 at 7:46 PM

Open Choice gives the option to publish under the subscription model or publish Open Access, which increases the visibility of your research.

A large number of subscription based journals are in the Springer Open Choice program and offer the option to publish your article Open Access. If you want more information and a list of “hybrid journals”, please check Fully open access journals and hybrid journals.

Upon acceptance of an article the corresponding author will receive a link to our Springer Nature author platform where they can sign the Copyright Transfer Statement online and indicate whether they wish to order Open Access and printing of figures in colour. Once that has been completed, the article will be processed and an email with a link to the Springer Nature eProofing tool will be sent to the corresponding author.

  • Publishing Open Access articles involves an open access publication fee (APC) which varies per journal (a full list is available on our Open Access Journals and Books webpage). For APC funding options, please check Funders for Open Access articles and books. You can choose to pay by credit card or to receive an invoice. For more information on payments, please check our APCs Payment Options
  • The open access publication fee does not replace any other existing journal publication costs, which are billed separately (e.g. surcharges for colour figures in print, oversized articles, etc.). You will find more information on that on the respective journals homepage.

Reprints & Permissions

Self-archiving

Self-archiving

Modified on: Tue, 15 Jul, 2025 at 7:51 PM

If your article is published under a subscription model, Springer Nature permits you to self-archive the accepted manuscript (AM), on your own personal website and/or in your funder or institutional repositories, for public release after an embargo period.

The accepted manuscript (AM) is the version post-peer review, but prior to copy-editing and typesetting, and does not reflect post-acceptance improvements, or any corrections.

When sharing AM you will need to make sure that it is done in accordance with our terms of use.

For more detailed information, please refer to Self-archiving of papers published via the subscription route section on our Open access policies for journals page.

Use of an open access article

Use of an open access article

Modified on: Wed, 6 Nov, 2024 at 2:57 AM

Most open access articles are made available under the Creative Commons Attribution (CC-BY) license, which means they are accessible online without any restrictions and can be re-used in any way, subject to proper attribution (which, in an academic context, usually means citation).

Please do check the license type of any article and how it can be used by accessing the Copyright information at the bottom of the article.

For more information about copyright and licensing for each of our open access brands, please see the links below:

  • BMC
  • SpringerOpen
  • Nature Portfolio
  • Palgrave Macmillan
  • Springer

Use of an open access figure or table

Use of an open access figure or table

Modified on: Thu, 30 Jul, 2020 at 7:18 PM

Reproduction of figures or tables from any open access article is permitted free of charge and without formal written permission from the publisher or the copyright holder, provided that:

  • the figure/table is original
  • the publisher is duly identified as the original publisher
  • the proper attribution of authorship and the correct citation details are given as acknowledgment

Research Data Sharing

Correctly citing and referencing your dataset for maximum impact

Correctly citing and referencing your dataset for maximum impact

Modified on: Tue, 3 Oct, 2023 at 8:43 PM

Where datasets are hosted in public repositories that provide datasets with persistent identifiers (such as Digital Object Identifiers (DOIs) or accession codes), we encourage formal citation of these datasets in reference lists. This allows your dataset to be more easily discovered by readers, search engines and citation indexing services.

Citations of datasets, when they appear in the reference list, should include the minimum information recommended by DataCite. These are: Creator, Title, Publisher (i.e., repository), Identifier, Publication Year. These references should also follow journal style.

For example:

Hao, Z., AghaKouchak, A., Nakhjiri, N., Farahmand, A. Global Integrated Drought Monitoring and Prediction System (GIDMaPS) Data sets. figshare. dx.doi.org (2014)

Barbosa, P., Usie, A. and Ramos, A. M. Quercus suber isolate HL8, whole genome shotgun sequencing project. GenBank identifiers.org (2018).

For more information, please email researchdatahelpdesk@springernature.com.

Data my journal expects me to share

Data my journal expects me to share

Modified on: Thu, 6 Nov, 2025 at 12:39 PM

Research data can take many forms across the fields of research covered by journals and books published by Springer Nature. Research data include any recorded factual materials that are used to produce the results in digital and non-digital form. This includes tabular data, code, images, audio, documents, video, maps, raw and/or processed data.

Research data refers to data produced by the authors (“primary data”) and data from other sources that are analysed by authors in their study (“secondary data”). However, you should not re-share secondary data. Instead, you should reference it as you would a literature reference.

The data sharing policies of our journals in general concern the minimal dataset that supports the central findings of a published study. For more information, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Find a data repository for my data

Find a data repository for my data

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 9:15 AM

If you are unsure where to deposit your data, we provide data repository guidance. You can also consult the repository indexing services re3data.org and FAIRsharing.org. Alternatively if your university, funder or institution provides a repository for their affiliated researchers, you may choose to deposit your data there.

We recommend using an appropriate discipline-specific repository where possible. Please note that our journals support community mandates for data sharing where applicable. If you are publishing in the life sciences or earth sciences, then mandatory data deposit to specific repositories may apply. For more information, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

My research didn’t generate data or I reused existing data

My research didn’t generate data or I reused existing data

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 9:30 AM

If your research did not generate or reuse research data, then your journal will not require you to share data as a condition of publishing. This may be the case for Review Articles, though most other article types will be supported by some form of data. Two quick checks you can perform: if your article mentions any summary statistics (means, standard deviations, etc), then your data are what you derived these from; if your article includes graphs, your data are what underlie these. If your research did not generate data, you should state this in your data availability statement.

If you reused data in your study, you should add this to your reference list, along with a description and citation in your data availability statement.

Whether or not you have data For more information, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Publishing articles that describe datasets

Publishing articles that describe datasets

Modified on: Tue, 26 Nov, 2024 at 12:36 PM

Data articles are peer-reviewed, citable papers that describe research data. In contrast to other article types, they do not contain theories, results or conclusions.Data articles enhance visibility and potential for reuse of the research data. They allow authors to get published, citable credit for their work in generating research data, and allows readers to focus on the data without being swayed by results.

The datasets themselves must be freely accessible to readers and accessible during the peer-review process. Publishing a data article does not prevent an author from publishing research papers using that data at a later date.

A number of Springer Nature journals offer some form of data article (for example, BMC Genomic Data and the Discover Journal Series), and we publish two dedicated broad scope data journals: Scientific Data and BMC Research Notes.

For information on other subject specific journals publishing data articles, please look at the article types in the journal’s Instructions for Authors. For further information or enquiries about data publishing, please researchdatahelpdesk@springernature.com.

Publishing data in a repository

Publishing data in a repository

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 9:32 AM

It is best practice to share your research data in a repository, and an increasing number of journals require you to do so. However, not every journal requires data to be deposited in a repository in order to publish a paper - some allow others methods of sharing.

You should check the Instructions for Authors of your chosen journal, where the Research Data policy outlines the specific requirements.

Please note that our journals support community mandates for data sharing where applicable. If you are publishing in the life sciences or earth sciences, then mandatory data deposit to specific repositories may apply. For more information, pleasesubmit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Release of research data ahead of manuscript publication

Release of research data ahead of manuscript publication

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 9:38 AM

Many Springer Nature journals and imprints, including the Nature journals and BMC journals, have established policies on data release ahead of publication. These policies make it clear data sharing will not be considered prior publication. Please check the Instructions for Authors of your chosen journal for further information.

Additionally, many data repositories can embargo your data or store them privately until you choose to release them. This can also facilitate the peer review of your data. Contact your chosen repository to find out if they support this.

Springer Nature strongly supports immediate and early sharing of research data in public health emergencies.

For more information, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Select the right licence for your data

Select the right licence for your data

Modified on: Tue, 9 Apr, 2024 at 5:24 PM

Where data are freely available online, we recommend a licence that enables the maximum potential for reuse, such as the Creative Commons CC0 public domain dedication waiver. Application of additional conditions (for example, non-commercial or share-alike) may have unforeseen effects, such as stopping users combining your dataset with other data that have different licences, or preventing companies using your data to develop beneficial products.

Springer Nature does not claim intellectual property rights for datasets connected to published papers. For more information, please email researchdatahelpdesk@springernature.com.

Sensitive data or data about human participants: sharing expectations

Sensitive data or data about human participants: sharing expectations

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 9:41 AM

Our journals acknowledge that reasonable restrictions on data availability may be necessary to protect human privacy, biosafety or to respect terms of use for data obtained under licence from third parties (for example, company proprietary data).

We encourage you to attempt anonymisation of data containing identifiers of human participants. If sufficient anonymisation is not possible, certain repositories support controlled access. Our repository guidance contains several suggestions.

If your data cannot be shared openly, you should include a data availability statement which describes why access to the data may be limited.

For more information, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Software and code sharing

Software and code sharing

Modified on: Thu, 20 Nov, 2025 at 3:37 PM

Springer Nature now maintains a unified company policy on code sharing. Submission to this journal implies that all relevant new code will be made freely available to any researcher wishing to use it, provided that such sharing does not compromise participant confidentiality. All journal articles will now feature a Code Availability section and authors will be encouraged to share code publicly, using permanent identifiers, and citing code they have used. Authors are encouraged to deposit their code in a stable, publicly accessible repository and to include appropriate documentation to faciltate re-use. For more information regarding the company-wide code sharing policy, please refer to the press release of Springer Nature’s unified open code policy.

Individual journals may include further requirements or guidance regarding software and code. The journals with existing code-sharing policies will see no changes. Authors should consult the Instructions for Authors or Submission Guidelines for their chosen journal to ensure compliance with any journal-specific expectations.

For journals without an existing policy, a gradual implementation will occur via integration into Snapp (Springer Nature’s Article Process Platform) providing a simplified and singular workflow for authors to manage their article submissions with the publisher. For more information, please email researchdatahelpdesk@springernature.com.

Write a data availability statement for a paper

Write a data availability statement for a paper

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 9:48 AM

Your data availability statement should describe how the data supporting the results reported in your paper can be accessed. This includes both primary data (data you generated as part of your study) and secondary data (data from other sources that you reused in your study). If the data are in a repository, include hyperlinks and persistent identifiers for the data where available.

If your data cannot be shared openly for any reason, for example to protect study participant privacy, then this should be explained in the data availability statement. If accessing or reusing your data is subject to any conditions of use or restrictions, these should also be described in the data availability statement.

Go to our data availability statement resource page for more information on writing data availability statements, and examples for different research areas. For more information, please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased to direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Retrospective changes

Make an article open access retrospectively

Make an article open access retrospectively

Modified on: Tue, 15 Jul, 2025 at 8:43 PM

If your article was published in a Springer Nature hybrid journal, it will, in most cases, be possible for your article to be made open access retrospectively if you wish to do so. For further details please see our policy on retrospective open access.

In order to place your order, please access our order form.

Should we be unable to accommodate your request, please note that you can still make the accepted version of your article (post-review, pre-copyediting) openly accessible in a repository six months after publication, in accordance with our self-archiving policy.

Order Retrospective Open Access

Order Retrospective Open Access

Modified on: Tue, 24 Feb, 2026 at 4:19 PM

The Order Retrospective Open Access form allows you to order open access for an article that has already been published. By opting for open access, your article will become freely available to all readers worldwide. It will also be automatically sent to PubMed Central and Europe PubMed Central if the article meets the PMC deposition guidelines; full details of our deposition policies are found under the “Self-archiving, manuscript deposition, and digital preservation” information on the Open Access Journal policies page.

Please note that not all article types are eligible for Open Access, and the eligible article types differ between journal. If your article type is not primary research, please visit the publishing model information on the relevant journal’s page to determine if your article type is eligible.

Also note that articles published Open Access retrospectively are not eligible for Transformative Agreements.

After submitting the form you may choose to receive an Open Access License to Publish (OA LtP) Form that needs to be signed and returned, but please be aware that resigning your Licence to Publish is no longer required. If you order open access for your article, you will be charged an article-processing charge (APC). The invoice will be sent to you after the license to publish document has been returned.

We will update the metadata and full text of the original article, which means that copyright, license and open access information in the article will be updated.

Please note that it is not possible to cancel this order and ensure you read our Retrospective open access for transformative and hybrid journals policy (under the ‘Other OA policies’ section) before submitting your request.

Post-publication journal corrections

Post-publication journal corrections

Modified on: Thu, 4 Dec, 2025 at 2:20 PM

Immediately upon publication of a paper, the published version becomes the official record, and it is then pushed to more than 100 distributing partners. As a result, changes that are made post-publication must be made through a formal correction process and may not always be possible.

Springer Nature will usually only issue formal corrections when the error fundamentally affects the scientific content of the paper or the publication record, please see an example of our corrections policies at the following link: Editorial and publishing policies | Scientific Reports

If an error is found in a published article (typos, author details, affiliations, or scientific corrections), please contact us by submitting a ticket. Please provide the following information, all of which is required to assist you. If your article requires multiple changes, please annotate a PDF version or screenshot of your published article and outline where the errors are in the text, as well as what the correct text should say. Where the changes are to figures, tables, or supplemental material please include corrected versions of these as separate files.

  • Title:
  • DOI*:
  • Corresponding author name & email address:
  • Error Description*:
  • Justification for correction*: Please note that, if the request does not comply with our policy, it will not be processed as a correction.

Royalties

Author Royalties: Declaration of status

Author Royalties: Declaration of status

Modified on: Tue, 26 Nov, 2024 at 10:13 AM

Depending on your country of citizenship, it may be a requirement to submit certain tax forms. Shortly after a new book gets published, our financial department will seek to get in contact with royalty recipients via email to obtain / confirm address and payment information. The email will contain instructions on how to submit tax related forms needed for the royalty payment. Once we receive all the required documentation you should expect to receive a royalty statement within one cycle after your title is published.

If your book has already been published and you have not heard from us please send an email to our globalroyaltysupport@springernature.com.

However; if you are a journal author and need to contact us regarding Declaration of status concerns, please send an email to our globaljournalsupport@springernature.com.

One of our Representatives will research the matter and will follow-up with you as soon as possible.

Obtain royalty payments / royalties

Obtain royalty payments / royalties

Modified on: Wed, 4 Dec, 2024 at 10:23 AM

Royalty payments are made based on the terms specified in the publishing agreement. Shortly after a new book gets published, our financial department will seek to get in contact with royalty recipients via email to obtain / confirm address and payment information. The email will contain instructions on how to submit relevant information needed for the royalty payment. Once we receive all the required documentation you should expect to receive a royalty statement within one cycle after your title is published. Cheque payments are released about once a month. There could be a delay when opting for this method of payment.

If your book has already been published and you have not heard from us please send an email to us at: globalroyaltysupport@springernature.com. Please include the title of the book and ISBN.

However; if you are a journal author and need information regarding your royalties, please send an email to us at: globaljournalsupport@springernature.com. Please include the article name and DOI.

One of our Representatives will research the matter and will follow up with you as soon as possible.

Payment of royalties via sub-right sales

Modified on: Fri, 4 Oct, 2019 at 6:57 PM

We will include all sales activity, including licensing, translations, and permissions on the royalty statement that we distribute on a cyclical basis. If you have received a notice from Springer Nature that your title has been translated, and you do not see this activity reported on your statement please send an email to us at: globalroyaltysupport@springernature.com. Please use this email address if you an author of a book title.

However; if you are a journal author and need to contact us regarding the above related royalty concerns, please send an email to us at: globaljournalsupport@springernature.com.

One of our Representatives will research the matter and will follow-up with you as soon as possible.

SNAPP login issues

SNAPP login issues

Modified on: Thu, 18 Dec, 2025 at 12:55 PM

If you are facing login issues with our submission system (SNAPP) as a the submitting author and you have not forgotten your password, it is likely due to one of the following reasons:

  1. Corresponding and Submitting Authors are not the same person

Please note that the amendment and revision pages in our submission system are only available to the Submitting Author. Please make sure you as the Submitting Author are logging in with the initial email address.

If you want to change the Submitting Author, please contact the journal directly by locating the respective journal contact (see last paragraph).

  1. Submitting Author uses different email addresses

Please note that the email address used during the submission has to be used throughout the whole process. If you want to change the email address, please refer to Change email name or email address.

  1. An outdated link was clicked

Please make sure that you are accessing the correct link from the most recent email received by our submission system. Links to previous stages will not be accessible anymore.

If the login issue persists or you want to change the submitting author, please go to Find publishing contacts for Springer Nature journals.

Submitting and Corresponding Author roles on SNAPP

Submitting and Corresponding Author roles on SNAPP

Modified on: Thu, 10 Jul, 2025 at 2:51 PM

One or more authors are assigned as Corresponding Authors to act on behalf of all co-authors and ensure that questions related to the accuracy or integrity of any part of the work are appropriately addressed.

Corresponding Authors is responsible for the following requirements:

  • ensuring that all listed authors have approved the manuscript before submission, including the names and order of authors;
  • managing all communication between the Journal and all co-authors, before and after publication*;
  • providing transparency on re-use of material and mention any unpublished material (for example manuscripts in press) included in the manuscript in a cover letter to the Editor;
  • making sure disclosures, declarations and transparency on data statements from all authors are included in the manuscript as appropriate (see above). *The requirement of managing all communication between the journal and all co-authors during submission and proofing may be delegated to a Submitting Author. In this case please make sure the Corresponding Author is clearly indicated in the manuscript.

If applicable, the Submitting Author will be asked to indicate their interest in an APC waivers and discounts on SNAPP on behalf of the Corresponding Author. As it is their responsibility, the Corresponding Author will receive an email after submission, wherein they are asked to provide evidence to support their request. For more information, please check our APC waivers and discounts policy.

What is SNAPP?

What is SNAPP?

Modified on: Wed, 30 Oct, 2024 at 2:50 PM

SNAPP is Springer Nature’s Article Processing Platform.

It provides a streamlined, easy-to-use experience for our authors, editors and reviewers when taking manuscripts from submission through peer review to acceptance.

We work closely with the academic community to refine the user experience and introduce new features – to make Snapp faster, more useful and easier to use.

Additional information is available on the Snapp Springer Nature’s editorial management system page.

Change my name or email address

Change my name or email address

Modified on: Fri, 8 Aug, 2025 at 6:05 PM

Once logged in to your Springer Nature Profile, click the ‘Account’ icon in the upper right corner of the screen. From the drop-down menu, select ‘Manage your account’ to update your stored name and address. Under ‘Personal details’, choose the option you wish to update and follow the on-screen instructions. You’ll be asked to verify changes via email confirmation links.

Note: Making changes to your name or email address here does not affect your subscription details. To update subscription-related information, go to the ‘Subscriptions and Purchases’ link via the ‘Account’ icon.

In addition, if your account is linked to a Third-party account such as Google or ORCID, you will not be able to change your email until it’s unlinked. To unlink:

  • Go to ‘Manage my account’
  • Click ‘Linked accounts’
  • Select ‘Unlink’ After a few moments, your account will be unlinked. You can then return to ‘Manage your account’ and update your email address.

Tip: You may need to log out and back in to see your updated account details reflected in your overview.

If you have any problems, please contact our Customer Service Team.

Changing your address for print subscriptions

Changing your address for print subscriptions

Modified on: Mon, 21 Jul, 2025 at 7:49 AM

Personal subscribers may change their delivery address at any time via the Subscriptions and purchases page once logged into their Account, and then logging into your account:

Your account looks like the below:

  1. Select Subscriptions and purchases

Here you will be able to view your digital bookshelf, claim online access to Nature subscriptions, change your address, access Springer+ or Nature+ and edit your email communications.

  1. Select change your subscription address

This will display the shipping address details associated with your subscription. If you have opposing Billing and Shipping addresses, please contact us directly to request your changes.

  1. Select Update Address

This page only contains your subscription address i.e. shipping address for your printed Issues. You will be able to make amendments to the Country, Region, Street 1&2 lines, House number, Suite/ Apartment or Unit Number, Postal code and City.

  1. Once the new details have been entered, select Update Address.

Confirmation that the details have been updated will display once they have been saved. As we print our address labels in advance, it may take 2-3 weeks for the new address to reflect on the shipping labels. If the address does not save, check the details you have entered or contact us directly for further assistance.

Institutional and corporate subscribers

Institutional and corporate subscribers should contact our Customer Service team with the information listed below.

  • Customer ID
  • title of the journal(s) you are subscribed to
  • the current delivery address
  • the new delivery address and effective date Please see our Claiming an Issue support page in regards to missing issues impacted by your move.

Forgotten Springer Nature Profile password

Forgotten Springer Nature Profile password

Modified on: Thu, 14 Aug, 2025 at 2:06 PM

To recover a forgotten Springer Nature Profile password for a range of our platforms and sites referenced within About Springer Nature Profile, enter your email address on the Springer Nature Profile login page. If your email address is recognised you will be prompted for your password. Click the ‘Forgot your password?’ link if you require a password reset.

Hint: Ensure your caps lock key is not on when entering your email and password details.

You will then receive an email with further instructions for resetting your password from ‘noreply@springernature.com’. If you don’t receive anything in about 10 minutes, check your junk mail or spam folder. However, if you have any further problems, contact our team by using our Submit a ticket: Springer Nature Support form.

Note: If the inbox of your registered email address is inaccessible for SNAPP [our submission system], please contact your journal directly.

Manage Multiple Email Addresses on Your Springer Nature Account

Manage Multiple Email Addresses on Your Springer Nature Account

Modified on: Mon, 10 Aug, 2026 at 2:02 PM

  • Keep Your Account Accessible and Flexible
  • What are the benefits?
  • How many email addresses can I add?
  • Adding an Email Address
    • Important
  • Choosing Your Primary Email Address
  • What happens when I change my Primary Email Address?
  • Removing an Email Address
  • When can’t I remove an email address?
  • Replacing an Existing Email Address
    • Frequently Asked Questions
Keep Your Account Accessible and Flexible

We’ve introduced a new way to manage your account by allowing you to link multiple email addresses to a single Springer Nature account.

Whether you’re changing jobs, using different email addresses for work and personal activities, or simply want more flexibility when signing in, you can now manage all your email addresses in one place.

What are the benefits?

By adding multiple email addresses to your account, you can:

  • Sign in using more than one email address.
  • Keep access to your account if you change employer or email provider.
  • Manage work and personal email addresses from a single account.
  • Choose which email address receives Springer Nature communications.
  • Reduce the need to create multiple accounts.
How many email addresses can I add?

You can normally add up to five email addresses to a single account.

In some situations, such as after account merges or when using linked sign-in services like Google or ORCID, your account may contain more than five associated email addresses.

Adding an Email Address

To add a new email address:

  • Sign in to your account.
  • Go to Manage Account.
  • Open the Email Addresses section.
  • Select Add Email Address.
  • Enter the email address you would like to add.
  • After the email is added. the status for the new email will show “verify”
  • Check your inbox for a verification email.
  • Follow the instructions in the email to verify ownership of the address. Once verified, the new email address will be available for sign in and account management.
Important
  • Every email address must be verified before it can be used.
  • Verification links are valid for 48 hours.
  • Email addresses that are already linked to another Springer Nature account cannot be added.
Choosing Your Primary Email Address

One email address on your account will always be designated as your Primary Email Address.

This email address is used for:

  • Account communications
  • Service updates
  • Notifications
  • Password and account-related messages You can change your Primary Email Address at any time by selecting another verified email address on your account.
What happens when I change my Primary Email Address?

Changing your Primary Email Address only changes where we send communications.

Your account remains the same and your existing sign-in methods continue to work, including:

  • Password sign-in
  • Institutional sign-in
  • Google sign-in
  • ORCID sign-in
Removing an Email Address

If you no longer need an email address on your account, you can remove it.

To remove an email address:

  • Go to Manage Account.
  • Open Email Addresses.
  • Select Remove next to the email address.
  • Confirm your choice. Once removed, you will no longer be able to sign in using that email address.
When can’t I remove an email address?

The Remove option may not be available if:

  • It is the only email address on your account.
  • The email address is linked to Google sign-in.
  • The email address is linked to an active ORCID connection.
  • The email address has not yet been verified. If an email address is currently receiving Springer Nature communications, you’ll be asked to choose another communication email address before it can be removed.
Replacing an Existing Email Address

If you have multiple email addresses and want to replace one of them, simply:

  • Add the new email address.
  • Verify the new email address.
  • Remove the old email address. This ensures you maintain uninterrupted access to your account.
Frequently Asked Questions

Do I need to verify every email address?

Yes. Every email address added to your account must be verified before it can be fully used.

Can I sign in using any verified email address on my account?

Yes. Once an email address has been verified and added to your account, it can be used to access your account.

Can I add an email address that already has a Springer Nature account?

No. An email address that is already associated with another account cannot be added as an additional email address. In this scenario the accounts would need to be merged

Will changing my Primary Email Address affect how I sign in?

No. Your existing sign-in methods remain linked to your account.

What should I do if my verification link has expired?

If more than 48 hours have passed, you’ll need to start the process again and request a new verification email.

If you have any questions then please email customerservice@Springernature.com

Register on Nature.com

Register on Nature.com

Modified on: Thu, 27 Mar, 2025 at 1:06 PM

Registering a MyNature account

Anyone can register a MyNature account free of charge at any time by completing the new account form. The account provides heightened access to site features such as enrolling to Nature e-alerts for journal news or subject areas, managing your linked accounts and accessing the eBookshelf for digital Springer purchases.

Subscribers will need to register a MyNature account to access their online subscription content and can review their subscriptions or manage their account when logged in.

Once registered a confirmation email will be sent to the email address provided that will contain a link to activate your new account.

Benefits of registering
  • Email Alerts
  • You can select to receive regular Email Table of Contents [ETOCs] for journals that are of interest to you or alternatively, by subject matter. You can also choose to receive updates for Advertising, Nature Jobs or Nature Events.
  • Special Subscription Offers
  • You will need to ‘opt in’ via your registered account to receive emails pertaining to special offers and new product information.
  • Selected Free Articles
  • Occasionally, selected articles may be accessible for free to registered account holders. Such articles will be clearly marked as ‘FREE’ within the ETOCs and on the website Table of Contents. Please note: Articles will usually be free for a limited period of time only.
  • Selected Nature Portfolio Portals and Gateways
  • Link through to topical content via Nature Portfolio’s various gateways and portals.
  • Research Community
  • Explore the stories behind the latest research articles by joining the free Nature Portfolio and Springer Nature Research Communities. If you’d be interested in writing for the Communities, please email communities@nature.com.
  • Article Abstracts
  • View abstracts and purchase individual articles via Nature Portfolio’s electronic delivery service.
  • Search and post science events from around the world on Nature Events or download the digital edition of the Nature Events Directory for free.
  • Search and post jobs on the Nature Careers website.
  • Track, browse, view, and comment on Nature Blogs.
  • Nature Preceding content. Please note registered account is required in order to purchase individual articles through nature.com and access to the full content of the Nature Portfolio products require the appropriate subscription.

If you encounter any error messages or other problems during the registration process, please contact us and we will get back to you as quickly as possible.

  • Then, click on the link in the email to choose a new password. If you do not receive the email in your inbox within 10 minutes, please check your junk and spam folders. If you continue to have problems accessing the site, please log a support ticket and our Institutional Support team will endeavour to assist.

Inactive Springer Nature Link account

When this happens a new account activation email is then sent to your registered email address. This email will include an activation link to activate your account. Check if you have received this email (it may appear in your SPAM/junk mail folder). If the original email was not received; or otherwise is lost/missing, then please contact Institutional Support and we will send you a new one.

Information in the Springer Nature Researcher Profile is based on Springer Nature publication data and public data from a researcher’s ORCID profile.

How can I remove my ORCID data from my Researcher Profile?

Modified on: Wed, 18 Feb, 2026 at 8:37 PM

.If you don’t want Springer Nature to access your ORCID data, you can remove your ORCID iD from your Springer Nature profile. Please go to the Linked Accounts section in your Springer Nature account settings.

How do I get a Springer Nature Researcher Profile?

How do I get a Springer Nature Researcher Profile?

Modified on: Wed, 18 Feb, 2026 at 8:36 PM

Profiles are automatically created for researchers who have a Springer Nature publication and a Springer Nature account with a linked ORCID iD. When a researcher links their ORCID iD to their Springer Nature account, they authorise Springer Nature to access information in their ORCID profile set to Trusted parties. Springer Nature will only use information set to Public in your ORCID profile on the Springer Nature Researcher Profiles.

To register a new or existing ORCID iD please visit Submit a manuscript with your ORCID number.

How do I make a correction to my Springer Nature Researcher Profile?

How do I make a correction to my Springer Nature Researcher Profile?

Modified on: Wed, 18 Feb, 2026 at 8:32 PM

If you are the author,, you can add publications directly to your ORCID profile. Changes made by a researcher to their ORCID record will be reflected on your Springer Nature Researcher Profile within 24 hours.

For changes to be reflected in your Springer Nature Researcher Profile, please ensure that the default visibility settings for individual items are set to Public in your ORCID profile.

For more information on how to edit your ORCID record please visit ORCID support.

Modified on: Wed, 18 Feb, 2026 at 8:32 PM

We collect publicly available data categories such as:

  • identification data (which may include your name and ORCID iD)

  • publication data (DOIs, journal IDs).

Discontinued Journal Apps

Discontinued Journal Apps

Modified on: Thu, 24 Oct, 2024 at 10:49 AM

On behalf of its society partners, Springer Nature created and managed around 500 journal-specific apps which allowed users to read content from those journals on their Android and iOS mobile devices.

As a result of falling usage for these apps over the past few years, they have officially been discontinued since the end of January 2019. We have reallocated our resources to other projects that will benefit our journals.

Users will still be able to access the content on our websites which are now extremely well adapted for viewing on different mobile devices, and we have observed most users searching for content are directed to our content platforms Springer Nature Link and Nature.com from a Google search.

A list of the impacted journal apps is attached or click on this link.

Society Web Portal/HTTP Referrer Access Issues

Society Web Portal/HTTP Referrer Access Issues

Modified on: Mon, 22 Sep, 2025 at 11:15 AM

If your society has access set up via a member portal in their website, then it is quite likely using http referrer technology to gain access to the journal(s) in Springer Nature Link.

First, please check that you are correctly logged into your society’s website/portal and your subscription is up to date.

If you are experiencing access issues after correctly logging in, try some of the following:

Cache/Cookies

Stored cache/cookies in your web browser (e.g. internet explorer) have been known to cause access/authentication issues. In many cases, users find that the authentication process is restored if they clear cache/cookies from their browser and close and re-open the browser. They should then visit the society site first, before visiting Springer Nature Link, log in on the society site, and then use the content links there to access Springer Nature Link content. (The access problem seems to happen mainly when users have first visited Springer Nature Link, then the society site, before returning to Springer Nature Link via content links on the society site.)

HTTPS to HTTP

Internet Explorer in particular does not pass HTTP header information from secure to non-secure sites (HTTPS to HTTP). This causes access not to work in cases where the external site uses HTTPS. This is a security implementation of the browser and not something we can do anything about. If you are using internet Explorer, please try a different browser such as Firefox or Google Chrome.

Internet Security Software

In some cases, internet security settings can be preventing access. Examples of this would be security settings in web browsers, anti-virus/security suites such as Norton and/or firewalls. Sometimes these settings will have an option to block http referrers, if such an option is turned on, please make sure it has been turned off. Often adding link.springer.com… to a list of trusted sites will resolve this problem.

If after trying some of these options, you still don’t have access please contact us at societyservice@springer.com.

Springer Nature Link Referral URL set up checks

Springer Nature Link Referral URL set up checks

Modified on: Mon, 31 Mar, 2025 at 1:48 PM

The new default setting for most sites is strict-origin-when-cross-origin. If this is set then when users click through to Springer Nature Link we will only see the domain and not the full URL of the page you are coming from. If we have the full URL registered then we won’t grant access as the domain will not be enough to authenticate users.

  • Check Referrer Policy
  • Check Link to Springer Nature Link Content
  • Check document.referrer
Check Referrer Policy
  • Right click on the link itself that the user will be using to click through to our site - Inspect>Network>Headers (If no information can be seen then hit CTRL+R to generate results, which will then show you the Headers information. The result to click on, the website itself, is usually the result that is top of the list on the left)

If your referrer policy is strict-origin-when-cross-origin, please consider adjusting it to no-referrer-when-downgrade, as this will widen the scope of information sent through to us by your referring URL.

Check Link to Springer Nature Link Content
  • Right click on the link that will take the user to Springer Nature Link from your Conference site, and click on Inspect
  • Then go to ‘Elements’
  • Check the Anchor tag (highlighted in yellow in the screenshot below) <a …>
  • Ensure that the link to our content is https://
  • Check that there isn’t ‘no referrer’ set (as this would override the site’s Referrer Policy). If ‘no referrer’ is set, please adjust your settings so that it no longer appears.
Check document.referrer

You can check what referral information you are passing to us by clicking on the Springer Nature Link content URL that you have on your site.

When you come through to Springer Nature Link from that link:

  • Right click on the page, and go to Inspect, and then Console
  • Type in document.referrer after the > If you have ‘noreferrer’ set at the link level, or your site referrer policy is too strict, then the result will be blank, as ‘no referrer’ means that no referrer URL will be passed to us.

document.referrer

’’

You would also see the blank document.referrer if you are coming from an https:// site and the link to Springer Nature Link has been added as http://, or if your site is http:// and you are trying to link through to Springer Nature Link which is https://. Please ensure that both links are ‘https’.

Springer Nature Link Referral URL Troubleshooting

Springer Nature Link Referral URL Troubleshooting

Modified on: Tue, 14 Apr, 2026 at 4:21 PM

Protocols in SpringerProtocols

Protocols in SpringerProtocols

Modified on: Tue, 10 Mar, 2020 at 8:44 PM

Research protocols contain a list of instructions that need to be followed to carry out a certain experiment as well as a list of materials, equipment, caution steps, troubleshooting advice, calculations and data analysis tips.

Protocols in SpringerProtocols are written in a readily reproducible step‐by‐step fashion, opening with an introductory overview of the procedure, a comprehensive list of the materials and reagents needed to complete the experiment, and followed by a detailed procedure that is supported with a helpful notes section with tips and tricks as well as troubleshooting advice.

SpringerProtocols

SpringerProtocols

Modified on: Mon, 19 Jan, 2026 at 6:21 AM

SpringerProtocols is the largest resource of protocols for the life sciences, with over 58,000 tried and tested references. All protocols in the series are written in a tried and tested format praised by bench researchers, including list of materials, step-by-step instructions and troubleshooting advice.

SpringerProtocols comprises more than 40 years of protocols content and features the critically acclaimed series Methods in Molecular Biology, edited by John M. Walker.

Hosted on Springer Nature Link, SpringerProtocols content can now be accessed on Springer Nature Experiments, the only research solution designed and optimized for protocols and methods search.

Additional information can be found in here in our SpringerProtocols website. If you have any further questions, visit our Library Contacts page for a list of Springer Nature sales representatives.

Editorial process after submission

Editorial process after submission

Modified on: Wed, 20 Nov, 2024 at 9:08 AM

This article shows the editorial process after Submitting a manuscript to a journal.

Stage 1: Initial quality check

This stage includes checks on authorship, competing interests, ethics approval and plagiarism.

We oversee this process to ensure that your manuscript contains everything our editors and peer reviewers need to perform a fair and thorough assessment of your work.

Stage 2: Editor assigned

Once your manuscript passes the initial quality check, we assign it to a member of Editorial Board, who is an active researcher in your field.

If the editor is satisfied with your work, they will choose appropriate peer reviewers to evaluate your work, taking into account several factors including expertise, experience and apparent conflicts of interest.

Stage 3: Peer review

Reviewers will assess the technical soundness and scientific validity of your methods, analysis and interpretation, all of which must be appropriate, properly conducted, ethically robust and fully supported by the data.

For additional information, please check our peer review policies for Springer, BMC and Nature portfolio or visit the respective journal website.

Stage 4: Decision

The editor will then decide whether to accept your work as is, request minor or major revisions, or reject the paper due to unresolvable concerns. In case of a rejection, an article transfer will be considered.

For all subsequent steps, please check Next steps for publishing your article: What to expect after acceptance.

Locate submission instructions for a Springer journal

Locate submission instructions for a Springer journal

Modified on: Mon, 27 Oct, 2025 at 9:23 AM

Editorial contacts and other information for authors can be found on the dedicated home page for each journal.

You may locate the journal of interest on Springer Nature Link using the search box or via the Index. If you are unsure which journal you would like to publish with, we recommend our Journal Finder, as described in Find the right journal for your manuscript.

For most journal homepages you will find the following tools for manuscript submissions:

  • “Submission guidelines”: This will allow you to read the submission guidelines for the specific journal and to upload your manuscript files.
  • “Manuscript editing services” : Additional offers to increase the quality of your article.
  • “Ethics and Disclosures” about the journal.
  • “Open Access fees and funding”: information about Open Access publishing in the respective journal.
  • “Contact the journal”: relevant contacts for each step of the publication process. Please see
  • Find the right journal for your manuscript for a detailed guide.
  • “Submit manuscript” This will allow you to go directly to the submission portal link for the journal. For further assistance please submit the Request Form and we will be pleased direct your query to the relevant person/team within Springer Nature.

Submission missing

Different Email Address Used: If you have made a submission but do not see it in your account, it may be because you used a different email address for the submission than the one you are using to log in to your profile. In this case, please log in using the email address associated with your submission. Once logged in, you can update your email address in the account settings to your preferred one.

If you still need to get a (matching) account, please create one under register a new account, to track your submissions and check your impact.

Not the Corresponding or Submitting Author: If you have contributed to a manuscript but are not the corresponding or submitting author, the submission might not appear in your account. Please check with the corresponding or submitting author for more details as well as progress updates.

Submission Through Another System: If you have submitted your manuscript through a different system (e.g., not via Snapp, eJP, or EM), your submission might not be visible in your profile. For status updates, please directly login to the submission system used.

Not confirmed corresponding author status via the Snapp email: If you are the corresponding author that has submitted your manuscript in our Snapp peer review system, you will have received an email from Snapp. You must confirm that you are the corresponding author via the response outlined in the email in order for you to see your submission(s) in Snapp.

Exceeded concurrency limits on login page

Exceeded concurrency limits on login page

Modified on: Fri, 20 Sep, 2024 at 5:02 PM

Individual access will allow each user one concurrent login at any one time. A warning that you have exceeded your concurrency limit suggests that your login details are already in use - you may be logged in via another browser, computer and/or device.

Tip: Ensure that you use the ‘log out’ button to immediately terminate your login session. If you closed the browser instead of logging out, you may need to wait for 30 minutes for your session to disconnect from the server.

Please do not share your login details with your colleagues, friends or peers. This is paramount for the security of your personal data and for ensuring that you have unrestricted access to the site.

If you are using a shared computer we recommend that you do not use the ‘save password’ option as the next user of that PC could access your account and prevent you from accessing content on another PC.

Login steps if your institution subscribes to Nature Masterclasses

Login steps if your institution subscribes to Nature Masterclasses

Modified on: Tue, 2 Jun, 2026 at 8:13 AM

  • User guides and videos
  • If your institution has access via institutional E-mail Domain
  • If your institution has access via institutional IP range
  • If your institution has access via institutional Single Sign-On
  • If your lab/department has access via voucher codes
User guides and videos

You can view all PDFs and video guides for each access method on our Access Guides page. Each access method is also summarized below.

If your institution has access via institutional E-mail Domain
  • Register on the Nature Masterclasses platform using your institutional email address, or log in with an existing SN Account that uses this email.
  • Your access is linked to your email domain, so please ensure you use the correct address when registering.
  • Once you have confirmed your registration, you will automatically receive access to the courses included in your institution’s subscription.
If your institution has access via institutional IP range
  • Connect to your institution’s on‑campus network (wired, Wi‑Fi or VPN).
  • Register or log in on the Nature Masterclasses website.
  • Once registered and confirmed, your SN Account will be associated with your institution for 30 days, allowing you to access courses from any internet connection.
  • If you have been off campus for longer than 30 days since your last login, simply return to the Nature Masterclasses website while connected to your institution’s network to refresh your access.
If your institution has access via institutional Single Sign-On
  • Search for your institution on the Nature Masterclasses website, or the Nature Masterclasses China site, if applicable.
  • You will be redirected to your institution’s login portal. Sign in using your usual institutional credentials.
  • You will return to Nature Masterclasses and be asked to log in or register with your SN Account. Once registered or logged in, you will have access to your institution’s subscription.
  • Your SN Account will remain associated with your institution for 30 days.
    • If this association expires, repeat the steps above to relink your account via your institution’s SSO.
If your lab/department has access via voucher codes
  • Register or log in to the Nature Masterclasses platform.
  • If you are not already part of a subscription, you will see a voucher/code redemption box on the homepage.
  • Enter your voucher code to unlock your access. If you have followed the steps above and are still unable to access your desired course, please submit a query to our Nature Masterclasses Support team via our webform here.
